# D011 and D166 in-depth source index â€” checkpoint

**Review date:** 2026-10-23
**Status:** In progress; source saturation is not claimed.
**Scope:** Current canonical D011 and D166 domains, areas and role records; evidence index only.

## Evidence separation and source volume

Area existence, boundaries and legacy-group synthesis rely on area-level task/skill/activity evidence and area sources only. Role-level sources can justify an individual role's route into a defined area; they cannot create or define an area.

No fixed 15-sources-per-category quota. Completeness is measured by layer x task x item-category x maturity x source-family coverage, claim-specific independent corroboration for material claims, exact locators, counter-evidence, and a documented saturation point.

### Credibility checks for grey sources

Grey sources are included when relevant and assessed individually, not dismissed by category. Record publisher/accountability, named author or accountable organization, date/version, transparent method or source trail, scope and conflicts/access, inspectable claim-level content, and independent corroboration. Use grey literature for documented practice, employer tasks, practitioner methods, implementation examples and discovery; do not let self-description, marketing, forum votes, vendor claims, a single posting, or community curation alone establish truth, prevalence, universal requirements, area boundaries or graph dependencies. State the level at which each claim is credible and retain limitations.

## Source families and current findings

### Historical role identities resolved

All 34 snapshot-only titles have individual source-backed dispositions in `D011_D166_LEGACY_ROLE_SNAPSHOT_DISPOSITIONS_2026_10_23.json`. No historical title was added to a current route. Three additional D166 role candidates discovered during the search are documented separately and await canonical review.

The old D166 snapshot included food production engineer (alias: food engineer), food and beverage packaging technologist, and packaging production manager. These are explicitly excluded; the current D011 and D166 role libraries contain no food or packaging role titles.

- [R stats: Pearson, Spearman and Kendall Correlation Tests](https://stat.ethz.ch/R-manual/R-devel/library/stats/html/cor.test.html) â€” R stats manual cor.test; opened 2026-10-23. Documents Pearson product-moment, Spearman rank and Kendall tau tests, their estimands, inference and exact/asymptotic options. **Limit:** API documentation is not method-theory instruction or proof that any correlation is stable/predictive in financial data. Pearson and rank methods answer related but distinct association questions; sample dependence and time structure require separate treatment.
- [R stats: Pearson Chi-Squared Tests for Count Data](https://stat.ethz.ch/R-manual/R-devel/library/stats/html/chisq.test.html) â€” R stats manual chisq.test; opened 2026-10-23. Documents goodness-of-fit and contingency-table tests, expected counts, 2x2 continuity correction and Monte Carlo p-values. **Limit:** Chi-square is not a general-purpose numerical test or a substitute for regression; asymptotic validity and table design matter. It does not establish a finance-specific task route by itself.
- [R stats: Fisher's Exact Test for Count Data](https://stat.ethz.ch/R-manual/R-devel/library/stats/html/fisher.test.html) â€” R stats manual fisher.test; opened 2026-10-23. Documents conditional exact tests for contingency-table independence, exact/Monte Carlo options and limitations for larger tables. **Limit:** Fisher's exact test is conditional on fixed margins and can become computationally difficult for larger tables; it is not automatically preferable for every chi-square problem or every financial dataset.
- [R stats: Linear Models, Regression, ANOVA and ANCOVA](https://stat.ethz.ch/R-manual/R-devel/library/stats/html/lm.html) â€” R stats manual lm; opened 2026-10-23. Describes fitting linear models for regression, single-stratum ANOVA and ANCOVA, weighted fits and model contrasts; notes data/time-series alignment considerations. **Limit:** Software reference is not a complete inferential course. Correct use depends on design, dependence, error structure, coding and diagnostics; shared API does not make all procedures or results equivalent.
- [R stats: Generalized Linear Models, Including Binomial/Logistic Regression](https://stat.ethz.ch/R-manual/R-devel/library/stats/html/glm.html) â€” R stats manual glm; opened 2026-10-23. Documents specifying response distribution and link, including binomial responses and iterative weighted least squares fitting. **Limit:** Does not teach full assumptions, separation, calibration, causal interpretation or market suitability. A binary outcome does not by itself prove logistic regression is the only suitable classifier.
- [statsmodels: Linear Regression Model Family](https://www.statsmodels.org/stable/regression.html) â€” Official guide; opened 2026-10-23. Describes OLS, WLS, GLS, GLSAR, rolling OLS/WLS and quantile regression with error-structure and model documentation. **Limit:** Available APIs are not a recommendation hierarchy, finance-specific validation or evidence of universal industry use. - [statsmodels: Regression with Discrete Dependent Variables](https://www.statsmodels.org/stable/discretemod.html) â€” Official guide; opened 2026-10-23. Lists Logit/Probit for binary responses, multinomial logit for nominal outcomes, and Poisson/negative-binomial models for count outcomes. **Limit:** API availability does not prove that these models are interchangeable or appropriate for any target. Causal claims, rare events, overdispersion and prediction objectives need task-specific review.
- [scikit-learn: Model Selection and Evaluation](https://scikit-learn.org/stable/model_selection.html) â€” Official user guide; opened 2026-10-23. Covers cross-validation iterators, model/parameter selection, validation, and classification/regression scoring metrics. **Limit:** General-purpose documentation does not establish leakage-safe evaluation for financial time series. Random folds can violate temporal information order; use time-aware splits and finance-specific validation evidence.
- [scikit-learn: Cross-Validation for Time-Series Data](https://scikit-learn.org/stable/modules/cross_validation.html) â€” Official cross-validation guide; opened 2026-10-23. Explains why ordinary KFold/ShuffleSplit i.i.d. assumptions can be inappropriate under temporal dependence and describes TimeSeriesSplit evaluating future observations; equal-interval and splitter limitations are stated. **Limit:** Generic TimeSeriesSplit is not sufficient for every financial label, overlapping horizon, panel or strategy backtest; PBO, purge/embargo and leakage controls need task-specific evaluation. Does not validate any return forecast.
- [The Probability of Backtest Overfitting](https://escholarship.org/uc/item/4w1110bb) â€” Bailey, Borwein, LÃ³pez de Prado and Zhu (2017), Journal of Computational Finance 20(4), DOI 10.21314/jcf.2016.322; opened article record and paper. Defines PBO and combinatorially symmetric cross-validation for investment-strategy selection risk. **Limit:** Proposed framework with its own assumptions and use conditions; it does not replace forward holdout, economic transaction-cost analysis, or all other validation methods and is not a regulator requirement.
- [Empirical Asset Pricing via Machine Learning](https://doi.org/10.1093/rfs/hhaa009) â€” Gu, Kelly and Xiu (2020), RFS 33(5), 2223-2273; opened 2026-10-23. Comparative methodology, U.S. equities study, out-of-sample design and discussion of regression benchmarks, regularization, trees and neural networks. **Limit:** One empirical asset-pricing study on selected U.S. equity data, predictors and periods. It does not prove ML universally outperforms traditional methods, transfer to all markets/roles, or prescribe a dependency graph.
- [Asset Pricing and Machine Learning: A Critical Review](https://onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/full/10.1111/joes.12532) â€” Bagnara et al.; online 2022, Journal of Economic Surveys 38(1), 27-56 (2024 issue); open access. Groups asset-pricing ML research into regularization, dimension reduction, trees/random forests, neural networks and comparative analyses. **Limit:** Review scope is asset pricing and literature available through its review period; it does not cover all quantitative finance or establish universal recommendations.
- [arch 7.2: Volatility Processes and Alternatives](https://arch.readthedocs.io/en/stable/univariate/volatility.html) â€” Stable arch 7.2 documentation lists constant variance, ARCH, GARCH family, EGARCH, FIGARCH, HARCH, MIDAS-HARCH, APARCH, EWMA and RiskMetrics2006 processes; explains a volatility process is combined with a mean model. **Limit:** API availability is not proof a model is appropriate for a specific asset, forecast horizon or regulatory risk task. This software page is not a substitute for model assumptions, theoretical methods or empirical comparison.
- [arch 7.2: Univariate Volatility Model Specification and Forecasts](https://arch.readthedocs.io/en/stable/univariate/univariate.html) â€” Stable docs show high- and low-level specification, mean and volatility component choices, and analytic or simulation/residual-bootstrap forecasts where tractable; linked modeling guide uses financial returns and reports conditional volatility/forecast outputs. **Limit:** Software examples and supported forecasts do not determine a correct model, forecast loss, assumptions or finance task fit; tutorial data are illustrative and forecast quality must be assessed out of sample.
- **Credibility assessment â€” Linear and Nonlinear Econometric Models versus Machine-Learning Models: Realized-Volatility Forecasting:** High-value grey research source because it is a dated, versioned Federal Reserve working paper with a full methods/results paper, explicit markets, forecast protocols and multiple robustness/evaluation checks. It is preliminary author research rather than Board policy or peer-reviewed consensus; independently compare with peer-reviewed volatility research and other markets.
- [Linear and Nonlinear Econometric Models versus Machine-Learning Models: Realized-Volatility Forecasting](https://www.federalreserve.gov/econres/feds/linear-and-nonlinear-econometric-models-against-machine-learning-models.htm) â€” Rehim Kilic, FEDS 2025-061r1, August 2025, revised September 2026. Compares HAR, ARFIMA, threshold/smooth-transition/Markov-switching HAR with XGBoost and neural networks on S&P 500/40 U.S. equities; evaluates multiple horizons using MSFE, MAE, QLIKE, model-confidence sets, Dieboldâ€“Mariano tests, realized utility and VaR/ES. Results vary by horizon; ML sometimes improves HAR but does not systematically beat the broader econometric set. **Limit:** Working paper, not Federal Reserve policy or a final consensus; U.S. equity samples and chosen predictor sets/horizons constrain transfer. Compares linear and nonlinear ML models against HAR for ten global equity indices (2000â€“2021), at daily/weekly/monthly horizons; abstract reports no systematic ML outperformance, no single model dominates statistical/economic measures, and the preferred model varies with horizon/objective. **Limit:** Ten indices and a bounded historical sample; abstract-level findings do not establish a universal ranking and detailed study design should be inspected before fine-grained route/technique claims.

- **Credibility assessment â€” Forecasting Stock Return Volatility: GARCH, Implied Volatility and Realized Volatility Models:** Credible grey access record for a peer-reviewed article: the university repository identifies the refereed journal/version and DOI and provides an accepted manuscript, while the journal is the version of record. - **Credibility assessment â€” Modeling and Forecasting Realized Volatility:** Strong academic working-paper record with named authors, date, full citation, later peer-reviewed publication and a specific empirical design; the 2001 results are historical and market-bounded, so do not treat them as a current performance ranking.

- **Credibility assessment â€” Basel Framework MAR50: Credit Valuation Adjustment Framework:** Credible for the issuing body's stated framework and scope (Basel Committee on Banking Supervision / Bank for International Settlements); applicability depends on jurisdiction, adoption/status and edition date. Recorded claim limit: Basel prudential capital framework with jurisdictional adoption and in-force-version limits; it is not a universal derivative-pricing recipe or a claim that all CVA desks use identical models. Check the applicable current Basel implementation and local rules.
- [Basel Framework MAR50: Credit Valuation Adjustment Framework](https://www.bis.org/committees/bcbs/basel-framework/standard/mar/50/inforce/2022-01-01/published/2019-12-15) â€” MAR50.3 and MAR50.32: CVA uses discounted future exposure, market-implied PD term structures estimated from traded credit spreads, and market-consensus expected LGD; illiquid counterparties may use peer-based proxy curves under rating/industry/region criteria. The framework distinguishes market-implied default probability from observed real-world likelihood. **Limit:** Basel prudential capital framework with jurisdictional adoption and in-force-version limits; it is not a universal derivative-pricing recipe or a claim that all CVA desks use identical models. Check the applicable current Basel implementation and local rules.
- **Credibility assessment â€” An Empirical Comparison of Credit Spreads between the Bond Market and the Credit Default Swap Market:** Credible academic grey literature for its bounded conceptual framework and reported historical comparison: BIS identifies the working-paper series, authors, date and research design, and the paper supplies a transparent source trail. It is not itself a prudential rule or peer-reviewed version of record; its dated market results require later research and current contract-source corroboration.
- [An Empirical Comparison of Credit Spreads between the Bond Market and the Credit Default Swap Market](https://www.bis.org/publ/work160.pdf) â€” BIS Working Paper 160 (2004), theoretical framework and empirical comparison. Contrasts structural models (firm asset value and default boundary, Merton lineage) with reduced-form/intensity models (default as random stopping time); derives CDS valuation using risk-neutral survival probabilities and discounting, then compares bond and CDS spreads. **Limit:** Dated working paper and market sample; useful for conceptual model distinctions and historical empirical results, not current market calibration conventions, a universal model ranking, or proof that a single structural/intensity variant is best.
- **Credibility assessment â€” Measuring Portfolio Credit Risk: Modelling versus Calibration Errors:** Credible as an attributable central-bank research publication with a named study, abstract, classification and inspectable linked paper; its claims are limited to the specific model/data/application summarized. It is research evidence, not regulatory guidance or universal error hierarchy, and should be corroborated with current validation standards and independent studies.
- [Measuring Portfolio Credit Risk: Modelling versus Calibration Errors](https://www.bis.org/publications/measuring-portfolio-credit-risk-modelling-versus-calibration-errors) â€” BIS publication abstract for a model-based portfolio-credit-risk study: separates model-specification error from calibration error and reports that flawed calibration can materially affect measured portfolio credit risk in its application. **Limit:** The source page summarizes one model and dataset; the result does not establish calibration error always dominates specification error, or generalize across portfolios, model families and current market regimes. Inspect the full paper and later evidence before quantitative claims.
- **Credibility assessment â€” QuEP 5: Using Replaceable Pricing Engines in Option Implementations:** Credible grey technical material for the proposal's own design rationale and named QuantLib architecture: the author is identifiable as a project maintainer and the project hosts the proposal with code references. It is an engineering design note, not an independent benchmark or finance-method authority; use official current API docs and numerical-analysis research to support engine accuracy or selection.
- [QuEP 5: Using Replaceable Pricing Engines in Option Implementations](https://www.quantlib.org/quep/quep005.html) â€” QuantLib Enhancement Proposal by maintainer Luigi Ballabio describes instrument/pricing-engine separation and analytic, finite-difference and Monte Carlo option engines; argues instrument contracts should be decoupled from calculation implementations and notes engine-specific model structures. **Limit:** A project design proposal (not a standard or empirical performance comparison); it describes architecture and examples, not convergence/error rankings, universal engine suitability, or current API completeness.

- **Credibility assessment â€” Markowitz Portfolio Construction at Seventy:** Credible only for the bounded, inspectable claim identified here because the publisher is attributable (Stanford University authors / Journal of Portfolio Management); assess authorship, date, source trail, conflicts and independent corroboration before generalizing. Recorded claim limit: One research formulation and publication, with its own modeling assumptions; it does not establish universal optimality, a single best robust method or the absence of estimation/model risk. Inspect paper and code before detailed use.
- [Markowitz Portfolio Construction at Seventy](https://web.stanford.edu/~boyd/papers/markowitz.html) â€” Current paper page identifies Journal of Portfolio Management 50(8), 117â€“160 (July 2024), authors and reproducibility code. Describes extending mean-variance optimization with constraints/transaction costs and handling uncertainty in return-statistic forecasts through a convex formulation. **Limit:** One research formulation and publication, with its own modeling assumptions; it does not establish universal optimality, a single best robust method or the absence of estimation/model risk. Inspect paper and code before detailed use.
- [Quadratic Program Example: Long-Only Portfolio Allocation](https://www.cvxpy.org/examples/basic/quadratic_program.html) â€” Official CVXPY example formulates a portfolio allocation as a quadratic program balancing expected return and variance, with nonnegative weights and optimization constraints; demonstrates solution and dual values. **Limit:** A generic software example, not a full investment method or a guarantee that the objective/inputs suit a particular investor. Numerical solver output does not validate financial assumptions or expected returns.
- **Credibility assessment â€” PyPortfolioOpt: Portfolio Optimization Methods and Implementation:** Credible grey implementation evidence for the project's own code, documented feature set and public software citation: the repository is inspectable, version history and tests are available, and it cites a peer-reviewed JOSS article. This does not independently validate investment assumptions or comparative claims; corroborate model theory and performance with primary research and task-specific evaluation.
- [PyPortfolioOpt: Portfolio Optimization Methods and Implementation](https://github.com/PyPortfolio/PyPortfolioOpt) â€” Repository documentation and project citation identify classical efficient-frontier/MVO, Blackâ€“Litterman, covariance shrinkage, L2 regularization, hierarchical risk parity, semivariance/CVaR/CDaR and critical-line methods; the project cites its 2021 Journal of Open Source Software article. **Limit:** Project feature coverage and tests do not prove each model's financial validity, suitability, current market use, or comparative performance. Treat popularity/readme claims as project self-description; verify methods against original papers, current research and data-specific evaluation.
- [Honey, I Shrunk the Sample Covariance Matrix](https://www.ledoit.net/honey_abstract.htm) â€” Journal of Portfolio Management 30(4), 110â€“119 (2004). The authors compare the sample covariance with a data-driven linear shrinkage estimator and explain its effect on meanâ€“variance portfolio estimation error and realized portfolio performance. **Limit:** A historical empirical study and one estimator/benchmark setting; its abstract is not a universal recommendation. Reassess assumptions, market period, target, loss function, constraints and modern alternatives before applying to a current portfolio.
- [Nonlinear Shrinkage Estimation of Large-Dimensional Covariance Matrices](https://doi.org/10.1214/12-AOS989) â€” The Annals of Statistics 40(2), 1024â€“1060 (2012), open access. Studies high-dimensional covariance estimation; contrasts linear shrinkage toward a common target with nonlinear eigenvalue shrinkage and evaluates estimation performance in theory and simulation. **Limit:** Asymptotic/statistical results and simulations do not by themselves prove superior realized performance for every financial universe, return process, constraint set or objective. Check dependence, nonstationarity and portfolio-level out-of-sample metrics.
- [Quadratic Shrinkage for Large Covariance Matrices](https://doi.org/10.3150/20-BEJ1315) â€” Bernoulli 28(3), 1519â€“1547 (2022), open access. Proposes quadratic shrinkage of inverse sample eigenvalues, relates finite-sample Stein estimation to large-dimensional asymptotics, and compares with linear shrinkage under stated loss and simulation settings. **Limit:** The paper's asymptotic loss, simulations and assumptions do not establish universal portfolio superiority or suitability under every dependence, regime, constraint or transaction-cost setting.
- [Machine Learning and Portfolio Optimization](https://pubsonline.informs.org/doi/10.1287/mnsc.2016.2644) â€” Management Science 64(3), 1136â€“1154 (2018), published online 2016. Introduces performance-based regularization and cross-validation for meanâ€“variance and mean-CVaR optimization, compares with sample-average, L1/L2 regularization and equal weighting on three Famaâ€“French datasets. **Limit:** Empirical comparison covers particular datasets, formulations and benchmarks; results do not guarantee transfer to other markets, objectives, costs, regimes or live trading. The paper's proposed performance-based methods require independent reproduction and leakage-aware validation.
- [Asset Allocation with Factor-Based Covariance Matrices](https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S0377221725002127) â€” European Journal of Operational Research 325(1), 189â€“203 (2025). Compares factor-based covariance approaches, including PCA/PLS-derived latent factors and static/dynamic structures, for minimum-variance portfolios; reports risk-adjusted results after transaction costs within the study design. **Limit:** One empirical study with specific assets, data, benchmarks and transaction-cost assumptions; its results do not establish general outperformance or universal suitability. Distinguish factor exposure modeling from ML forecasting and validate on the intended universe and period.
- [Portfolio Optimisation via Strategy-Specific Eigenvector Shrinkage](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s00780-025-00566-4) â€” Finance and Stochastics 29, 665â€“706 (2025), open access. Develops a generalized Jamesâ€“Stein estimator for leading sample eigenvectors, using portfolio-constraint gradients to define a target subspace, and studies minimum-variance portfolios with finitely many linear constraints. **Limit:** A recent theoretical and empirical contribution tied to stated linear constraints and model conditions; not a general covariance estimator or evidence that the approach wins for arbitrary mandates. Requires replication and comparison to relevant baselines.
- [Shrinkage Estimation with Reinforcement Learning of Large Variance Matrices for Portfolio Selection](https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S2667305323000066) â€” Intelligent Systems with Applications 17 (2023), article 200181. Proposes policy-gradient and recurrent-policy-gradient agents to tune shrinkage intensity for large covariance matrices, with an empirical asset-allocation comparison on industry portfolios. **Limit:** A single proposed method and bounded empirical comparison; it does not establish robust superiority, safe deployment or transfer across markets. Compare with transparent linear/nonlinear shrinkage and simple baselines using time-aware validation and transaction costs.
- [Learning the Shrinkage Intensity: A Data-Driven Approach for Risk-Optimized Portfolios](https://academic.oup.com/jfec/article/24/2/nbag002/8512976) â€” Journal of Financial Econometrics 24(2), online 2026. Studies supervised policy learning for data-dependent covariance shrinkage intensities in high-dimensional minimum-variance portfolios; situates the contribution beside linear/nonlinear shrinkage and reinforcement-learning approaches and evaluates out-of-sample portfolio risk. **Limit:** Very recent paper and empirical design; the abstract's reported gains are author-reported and do not prove future/live-market superiority. Inspect full methods/data, benchmark leakage and regime assumptions, and independently replicate before making a student-facing performance claim.

Area sources are listed under each canonical area in the JSON ledger and come only from area task/skill/activity evidence.

#### Current area records

- **Quantitative Pricing and Financial Engineering** (`D011:quantitative-pricing-and-financial-engineering`): Use mathematical and computational models to value financial instruments and engineer financial products/strategies, including derivative valuation, numerical methods, calibration and model implementation.
  - [CFA Program Candidate Body of Knowledge](https://www.cfainstitute.org/programs/cfa-program/candidate-resources/cbok) â€” Quantitative Methods includes probability, regression, time series, AI/ML and big-data applications, backtesting/simulation/scenario analysis; portfolio topics include construction, execution, trading and market microstructure. Supports: Quantitative methods are used in investment analysis and portfolio decisions; systematic investment, execution and market microstructure form distinguishable applied concerns.
  - [Portfolio Mathematics (2026 Curriculum)](https://www.cfainstitute.org/insights/professional-learning/refresher-readings/2026/portfolio-mathematics) â€” Learning outcomes cover expected portfolio value, variance, standard deviation, covariance/correlation and shortfall-risk metrics; the reading connects quantitative portfolio mathematics to portfolio risk/return. Supports: Quantitative portfolio measurement and risk analysis are core, identifiable financial applications of mathematical methods.
  - [CFA Program Curriculum Overview](https://www.cfainstitute.org/programs/cfa-program/curriculum) â€” Curriculum structures quantitative methods, derivatives/risk management, portfolio construction, asset classes and specialized portfolio-management pathways as distinct topic areas. Supports: Financial valuation/risk and portfolio decision-making are related but distinguishable knowledge/application clusters.
  - [Active Equity Investing: Strategies (2026 Curriculum)](https://www.cfainstitute.org/insights/professional-learning/refresher-readings/2026/active-equity-investing-strategies) â€” Summary and learning outcomes distinguish quantitative/systematic from fundamental/discretionary investing; describes factor, statistical-arbitrage and market-microstructure strategies, and quantitative workflow including data processing, backtesting, evaluation, portfolio construction, risk and trading-cost models. Supports: Systematic quantitative investment/trading is a coherent application area distinct from general financial modeling and risk quantification.
  - [FRM Program Exams and Topic Areas](https://www.garp.org/frm/program-exams) â€” 2026 exam topics: Part I Foundations of Risk Management, Quantitative Analysis, Financial Markets and Products, Valuation and Risk Models; Part II Market Risk, Credit Risk, Operational/Integrated Risk, Liquidity/Treasury Risk, and Risk/Investment Management. Supports: Quantitative methods and valuation models form a distinct foundation, with applied financial risk areas such as market, credit and liquidity risk; professional material also connects risk and investment management.
  - [Valuation of Contingent Claims (2026 Level II Derivatives)](https://www.cfainstitute.org/insights/professional-learning/refresher-readings/2026/valuation-contingent-claims) â€” 2026 derivatives reading: contingent claims/options, model-based valuation and no-arbitrage links; forward/futures/swaps addressed in related curriculum. Recorded claim limit: Restricted advanced course with stated econometrics/asset-pricing prerequisites; course inclusion does not make every method compulsory for all roles or determine a fixed learner sequence.
  - [linearmodels 7.1: Panel, Instrumental-Variable and Asset-Pricing Model Documentation](https://bashtage.github.io/linearmodels/) â€” Version 7.1 docs opened 2026-10-23; documents fixed/random/first-difference/between/pooled/Fama-MacBeth panel estimators, IV2SLS, LIML, IV-GMM/CUE/JIVE, linear factor model/SUR estimators and weak-identification, exogeneity, overidentification and panel-specification tests. Supports: Concrete implementation map showing estimator alternatives and diagnostics for distinct panel, endogenous-regressor, GMM and empirical-asset-pricing tasks; clarifies that estimator choice entails identifying assumptions and specification checks. Credibility: Credible only for the bounded, inspectable claim identified here because the publisher is attributable (linearmodels open-source project); assess authorship, date, source trail, conflicts and independent corroboration before generalizing. Recorded claim limit: Maintainer documentation reports software scope and implementation, not unbiased results for a dataset, a complete methods textbook, universal preference, or proof of model assumptions. Verify theory, versions, data structure and inference design separately.
  - [Risk, Return, and Equilibrium: Empirical Tests](https://www.journals.uchicago.edu/doi/10.1086/260061) â€” Fama and MacBeth (1973), Journal of Political Economy 81(3), pp. 607â€“636; publisher record and abstract inspected 2026-10-23. Full text is subscriber-gated. Supports: Foundational two-pass empirical test of the relationship between average stock returns and risk, providing context for the later named Fama-MacBeth cross-sectional estimation procedure in asset-pricing research.
  - [Event Studies in International Finance Research](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35822194/) â€” El Ghoul, Guedhami, Mansi and Sy (2022), Journal of International Business Studies 53(2), pp. 344â€“364; PubMed record and indexed article abstract inspected 2026-10-23. Full text linked via PMC but direct fetch was bot-blocked. Supports: Reviews event selection, event/estimation windows, normal-return models, abnormal-return inference and short- versus long-horizon challenges in cross-country equity and fixed-income research; emphasizes model and international-market heterogeneity.
  - [Double/Debiased Machine Learning for Treatment and Structural Parameters](https://academic.oup.com/ectj/article/21/1/C1/5056401?login=false) â€” Chernozhukov et al. (2018), The Econometrics Journal 21(1), C1â€“C68; Oxford Academic article record/summary inspected 2026-10-23. Supports: A principled bridge from modern ML to econometric inference: flexible learners estimate high-dimensional nuisance functions, while Neyman-orthogonal scores and cross-fitting target low-dimensional causal/structural parameters under stated conditions.

  - [Computing Competencies for Undergraduate Data Science Curricula â€” ACM Data Science Task Force](https://dstf.acm.org/DSTF_Final_Report.pdf) â€” Accessible official task-force report (January 2021), 131 pages; its interdisciplinary curriculum integrates computer science, mathematics/statistics and information science foundations. **Supports:** Adjacent computational/statistical curriculum context for quant-finance computing and statistical-assistant learning. **Limit:** Does not analyze quantitative-finance occupations, establish employer task prevalence or make every competency mandatory for every role.
  - [Computer Science Curricula 2023 â€” ACM, IEEE Computer Society, and AAAI](https://csed.acm.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/11/CS2023-Report.htm) â€” The endorsed 2024 curriculum report separates required CS Core, recommended knowledge-area core and non-core topics; mathematical/statistical foundations support advanced areas including AI, and the report describes statistics, probability, linear algebra, ML and data-management competencies. **Supports:** Transferable computing and quantitative curriculum context for computational finance and statistical-assistant work. **Limit:** A computer-science curriculum is not a quantitative-finance task analysis and does not establish finance-specific duties, tool requirements or universal job prerequisites.
  - [Curriculum Guidelines for Bachelor of Arts Degrees in Statistical Science â€” Journal of Statistics Education / American Statistical Association community](https://jse.amstat.org/v10n2/tarpey.html) â€” 2002 proposed flexible statistics curriculum covering mathematics, core statistics and a substantive application area; article reports a workshop involving statisticians and nonacademic employers. **Supports:** Historical curriculum-design context for statistical-assistant and quantitative-analysis learning foundations. **Limit:** Dated curricular guidance, not finance-specific or current employer task evidence; it cannot establish present-day role requirements or sequence by itself.
  - [NIST Big Data Interoperability Framework: Volume 1, Definitions â€” U.S. National Institute of Standards and Technology (NIST)](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/SpecialPublications/NIST.SP.1500-1r2.pdf) â€” Revision 2 (2019) defines big-data concepts and terms within the NIST interoperability framework. **Supports:** Data-engineering terminology context that may help computational finance learners interpret large-scale data systems. **Limit:** Definitions framework only; it does not establish quantitative-finance role tasks, tool choices, analytic methods or individual competency requirements.
  - [DAMA-DMBOK2R Infographics â€” DAMA International](https://dama.org/dmbok2r-infographics/) â€” Official DMBOK 2nd Edition Revision diagrams/context diagrams for data-management knowledge areas. **Supports:** General data-management framework context relevant to quantitative research/data operations where data governance and lifecycle work is assigned. **Limit:** Framework diagrams are not finance-specific tasks, evidence of role prevalence or a credential; do not treat them as proof that a quant role owns enterprise data governance.
  - [IS 2010: Curriculum Guidelines for Undergraduate Degree Programs in Information Systems â€” Association for Computing Machinery (ACM) and AIS](https://www.acm.org/binaries/content/assets/education/curricula-recommendations/is-2010-acm-final.pdf) â€” Model curriculum guidance describes shared core content plus career-targeted electives and explicitly says the model guides rather than prescribes; ACM indexed text was inspected because direct retrieval was limited. **Supports:** Historical information-systems curriculum design context for data/technology-facing roles. **Limit:** 2010 information-systems curriculum, not current finance task evidence or a quantitative-finance role standard; its age and domain limit transfer.
  - [Certified Business Intelligence Professional (CBIP) â€” Transforming Data With Intelligence (TDWI)](https://tdwi.org/cbip) â€” Current credential overview covers business intelligence, analytics, data management, data architecture and data strategy certification areas, exam and renewal requirements. **Supports:** Optional broad BI/data-management credential context for roles explicitly involving analytics platforms or data architecture. **Limit:** Not a quant-finance credential or job requirement; credential availability does not show that quantitative researchers, traders or mathematicians need it.
  - [TDWI Analytics Maturity Model Assessment â€” Transforming Data With Intelligence (TDWI)](https://tdwi.org/pages/assessments/adv-all-tdwi-analytics-maturity-model-assessment.aspx) â€” Organizational self-assessment of analytics deployment maturity across business and IT capabilities. **Supports:** Organizational analytics-maturity context only; potentially relevant to enterprise analytics transformation consulting. **Limit:** An organizational diagnostic is not an individual skill, tool, method or role task and does not support a mathematician/quant analyst destination by itself.
  - [DAMA-DMBOK 2nd Edition Revision â€” DAMA International](https://dama.org/dama-dmbok-revision/) â€” Official page identifies the 2024 DMBOK 2.0 maintenance revision, its terminology/diagram/sequence clarifications and the continuing DMBOK 3.0 development effort. **Supports:** Current professional data-management framework context for data stewardship, governance and lifecycle concepts where relevant to financial data work. **Limit:** A general framework revision, not a quantitative-finance role analysis, job requirement or method for pricing/risk research.
  - [Actuarial Practice Areas â€” Institute and Faculty of Actuaries (IFoA)](https://actuaries.org.uk/practice-areas/) â€” Current professional-body map includes finance/investment, general insurance, risk management, AI/emerging technology and other actuarial practices; it distinguishes cross-practice themes from specialist practices. **Supports:** Professional practice-area context for actuarial/insurance-rating and investment-risk learning branches. **Limit:** An actuarial professional taxonomy, not a task survey for every quantitative-finance role; do not apply all actuarial practices to a statistical assistant or quant researcher.
  - [IAA Forums â€” International Actuarial Association (IAA)](https://actuaries.org/forums/) â€” Current forum directory describes knowledge-sharing communities for banking, data analytics, enterprise/financial risk and general insurance, among others; participation is through member associations/appointments. **Supports:** Professional-community and emerging-practice discovery for actuarial financial-risk and data-analytics contexts. **Limit:** Forum existence and remit do not establish individual job duties, technical method validity, a credential or required learner route.
  - [About the Casualty Actuarial Society â€” Casualty Actuarial Society (CAS)](https://www.casact.org/about) â€” Professional-body overview links to current actuarial education, examinations, syllabi and credential pathways for property/casualty actuarial practice. **Supports:** Source provenance and professional-learning context for insurance rating/reserving; the live syllabus is the stronger source for specific study content. **Limit:** The about page itself is not a detailed role-task analysis and does not establish that a quant-finance role requires CAS exams.
  - [FSA Flexible Pathway â€” Society of Actuaries (SOA)](https://fsa2025.soa.org/flexible-pathway/) â€” Official FSA curriculum structure allows four technical courses including a two-course sequence in one practice area or a cross-practice combination; general-insurance courses sequence GI 101 to GI 201/GI 301 with distinct learning outcomes. **Supports:** Concrete professional-curriculum example of a shared actuarial foundation followed by a selected specialization and prerequisite-aware, branch-specific course progression; useful for insurance-rating learning design review. **Limit:** Actuarial credential curriculum, not a quantitative-finance task survey or universal sequence; the linked catalog and rules are time/version dependent.

- **Credibility assessment â€” PID Longitudinal Controller:** High credibility for Autoware's implementation and assumptions; project-specific parameterization must be independently validated on the target vehicle.

Area sources are listed under each canonical area in the JSON ledger and come only from area task/skill/activity evidence.

#### Current area records

- **Vehicle Sensing and Sensor Calibration** (`D166:vehicle-sensing-and-sensor-calibration`): Acquire, synchronize, calibrate and preprocess sensor signals and maintain the sensor hardware/data interfaces used by automated-driving systems.
  - [Autoware Architecture Overview](https://docs.autoware.org/pr-803/design/autoware-architecture-v1/) â€” Architecture overview separates sensing, map, localization, perception, planning, control, and vehicle interface stacks; it describes replaceable modules and component boundaries. Supports: Supports separate component areas and warns that this architecture is a technical system decomposition, not a universal curriculum sequence.
  - [Sensing Component Design](https://docs.autoware.org/main/design/autoware-architecture-v1/components/sensing/) â€” Sensing abstracts sensor data formats and performs primitive preprocessing; lists camera, lidar, radar, GNSS/INS, IMU and ultrasonic inputs. Supports: Supports a distinct sensor systems, calibration, and data acquisition area upstream of perception and localization.
  - [Automated Driving Systems 2.0: A Vision for Safety](https://www.nhtsa.gov/document/automated-driving-systems-20-voluntary-guidance) â€” Voluntary ADS guidance identifies system safety, ODD, object/event detection and response, fallback, human-machine interface, cybersecurity, validation and other safety elements. Supports: Supports safety engineering and validation as a cross-cutting practice spanning ADS components.
  - [Calibrating Your Sensors](https://docs.autoware.org/main/tutorials/integrating-autoware/creating-vehicle-and-sensor-model/calibrating-sensors/) â€” Current integration tutorial and linked lidar-lidar, ground-plane/lidar, intrinsic-camera, lidar-camera and lidar-IMU calibration workflows; inspected 2026-10-23. Supports: Practical sensor calibration and sensor-to-vehicle/model integration tasks for AV sensing; connects sensor setup to downstream localization/perception use. Credibility: Credible as primary documentation of Autoware Foundation's own product, architecture or implementation claims; it is provider-authored, so corroborate field-wide claims, comparative performance and safety implications independently. Recorded claim limit: Autoware-specific workflows and supported hardware/configuration; does not establish that every sensor stack uses identical procedures or calibrations.

  - [Sync or Sink? The Robustness of Sensor Fusion Against Temporal Misalignment](https://ieeexplore.ieee.org/document/10568077/) â€” 2024 RTAS publisher abstract/metadata inspected 2026-10-23; direct page requires JavaScript. Studies camera-LiDAR 3D detection fusion under sensor time offsets and defines temporal-robustness evaluation types. Supports: Shows why timestamps, clock alignment and motion-aware compensation are upstream conditions for reliable multimodal fusion; contributes an evaluation question beyond static geometric calibration. Credibility: Peer-reviewed RTAS paper with publisher metadata, DOI and indexed abstract; suitable as a research lead on timing robustness, but source access limits detailed claims until full text is examined.

Supports: Maps alternative sensing and fusion choices: why combine complementary radar/camera observations, what representations are fused and where in the processing pipeline fusion occurs; relevant to interface design and downstream perception quality. Credibility: Peer-reviewed IEEE Transactions survey with named journal, issue, DOI and indexed abstract; credible as a research map, with full-text/method-specific claims still open due access limit.

## D011 named-technique comparisons and relationships

These source-backed comparisons describe technique distinctions, alternatives, complements and claim limits for later graph review. They are not canonical items, student-display approvals, or graph/sequence edges.

- **Correlation techniques: Pearson, Spearman and Kendall** â€” They are related association measures but not interchangeable alternatives: Pearson targets product-moment/linear association under its inferential assumptions, while Spearman and Kendall use ranks and answer rank-association questions. Pick according to the estimand, data scale, dependence structure and assumptions; finance time-series autocorrelation and nonstationarity still need separate handling. **Sources:** [D011-R-CORRELATION-TESTS](https://stat.ethz.ch/R-manual/R-devel/library/stats/html/cor.test.html) **Limit:** R documentation establishes implementation choices and stated assumptions, not finance-task prevalence or the suitability of an individual correlation screen.
- **Categorical/count inference: Pearson chi-square and Fisher exact test** â€” Both can test association in contingency tables, but their inferential setup differs. Pearson chi-square uses an asymptotic reference distribution (and offers Monte Carlo p-values); Fisher conditions on fixed margins and computes exact probabilities when feasible. Expected counts, table structure, margins and computational size guide comparison. Neither is a universal replacement for the other. **Sources:** [D011-R-CHI-SQUARE-TEST](https://stat.ethz.ch/R-manual/R-devel/library/stats/html/chisq.test.html); [D011-R-FISHER-EXACT-TEST](https://stat.ethz.ch/R-manual/R-devel/library/stats/html/fisher.test.html) **Limit:** These are count-data association/goodness-of-fit procedures, not continuous-outcome regression or numeric finance-return tests; domain relevance depends on a categorical/count task.
- **Linear regression, ANOVA and ANCOVA** â€” A one-stratum ANOVA or ANCOVA can be represented within a linear-model framework, while the question, factor coding, design, contrasts and inferential interpretation differ. ANOVA is not a separate universal prerequisite after regression; it is a related analysis for comparisons of means/model terms under a specified design. **Sources:** [D011-R-LM-ANOVA-ANCOVA](https://stat.ethz.ch/R-manual/R-devel/library/stats/html/lm.html); [D011-STATSMODELS-REGRESSION-FAMILY](https://www.statsmodels.org/stable/regression.html) **Limit:** Software documentation demonstrates shared model machinery, not equivalence of research questions or assumptions. Time-series and panel dependence require suitable covariance/error handling.
- **Regression choices by outcome form: OLS versus logistic/probit/count models** â€” OLS is a linear conditional-mean model commonly used for numeric outcomes; binary Logit/Probit models use a binary response and link; Poisson/negative-binomial models address count outcomes. The response scale and estimand drive the choice. These are model-family alternatives under different data-generating and decision questions, not tool substitutes or a single compulsory chain. **Sources:** [D011-R-LM-ANOVA-ANCOVA](https://stat.ethz.ch/R-manual/R-devel/library/stats/html/lm.html); [D011-R-GLM-BINARY-LOGISTIC](https://stat.ethz.ch/R-manual/R-devel/library/stats/html/glm.html); [D011-STATSMODELS-DISCRETE-RESPONSE](https://www.statsmodels.org/stable/discretemod.html) **Limit:** This index does not adjudicate causal interpretation, rare-event performance, overdispersion, or a specific finance dataset; each needs method and task-specific evidence.
- **Traditional econometrics and machine learning in asset pricing** â€” Finance research compares parsimonious regression benchmarks with regularization/dimension reduction and flexible tree/neural-network models. The reviewed study attributes some predictive gains to nonlinear predictor interactions unavailable to simpler specifications, but higher flexibility increases overfitting risk. Its U.S.-equity results are evidence that ML can extend familiar regression benchmarks for a defined task, not evidence of universal superiority or a required replacement. **Sources:** [D011-RFS-ML-ASSET-PRICING-2020](https://doi.org/10.1093/rfs/hhaa009); [D011-WILEY-ASSET-PRICING-ML-REVIEW-2024](https://onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/full/10.1111/joes.12532) **Limit:** Task, predictor set, period, market and evaluation design bound these findings; compare against robust baselines and current evidence before mapping method dependencies.
- **Model evaluation for financial prediction and strategy selection** â€” Generic ML model selection provides fitting, validation and cross-validation tools, but financial time-series validation must respect chronology; scikit-learn explicitly distinguishes time-dependent data from i.i.d. folds and provides future-only TimeSeriesSplit with conditions. Separately, PBO/CSCV addresses selection overfit among many backtested strategy configurations. These methods answer related but different validation questions and can complement an untouched out-of-sample test and economic cost analysis. **Sources:** [D011-SCIKIT-MODEL-EVALUATION](https://scikit-learn.org/stable/model_selection.html); [D011-SCIKIT-TIME-SERIES-SPLIT](https://scikit-learn.org/stable/modules/cross_validation.html); [D011-RFS-ML-ASSET-PRICING-2020](https://doi.org/10.1093/rfs/hhaa009); [D011-PBO-BACKTEST-OVERFIT-2017](https://escholarship.org/uc/item/4w1110bb) **Limit:** No single evaluation method proves a strategy will work live. Label overlap, information leakage, transaction costs, capacity, multiple trials and regime change require explicit handling. These notes are evidence for graph review, not graph edges.
- **Time-series mean dynamics and conditional-volatility models are separate, composable choices** â€” AR/ARIMA/VAR and related models describe conditional means or joint dynamics and handle trend, stationarity, seasonality and cross-series relationships. ARCH/GARCH-family models describe changing conditional variance, often attached to a mean equation after residual diagnostics show volatility clustering. They can be combined; GARCH is not a substitute for ARIMA or VAR, and an AR model is not a volatility model. Forecast target and diagnostics decide whether either layer is useful. **Sources:** [D011-STATSMODELS-TIME-SERIES-2026](https://www.statsmodels.org/stable/tsa); [D011-CFA-TIMESERIES-2026](https://www.cfainstitute.org/insights/professional-learning/refresher-readings/2026/time-series-analysis); [D011-ARCH-VOLATILITY-PROCESSES-2026](https://arch.readthedocs.io/en/stable/univariate/volatility.html) **Limit:** Software API and curriculum establish the model families and teaching context, not universal modeling practice. The choice and ordering depend on data frequency, target, assumptions and residual evidence; this is conceptual composition, not a learner prerequisite edge.
- **Conditional variance alternatives: ARCH/GARCH variants, EWMA and RiskMetrics** â€” ARCH/GARCH variants model conditional variance from past innovations and variance; asymmetric EGARCH, GJR/TARCH or APARCH forms can express sign/asymmetry or power responses, while FIGARCH/HARCH/MIDAS variants address different persistence/aggregation structures. EWMA/RiskMetrics are simpler parameterized or fixed-weight variance processes and may serve as baselines or operational alternatives. These choices answer a variance-estimation/forecast question; use residual diagnostics, distribution assumptions, horizon and evaluation loss to compare them. **Sources:** [D011-ARCH-VOLATILITY-PROCESSES-2026](https://arch.readthedocs.io/en/stable/univariate/volatility.html); [D011-ARCH-UNIVARIATE-MODELS-2026](https://arch.readthedocs.io/en/stable/univariate/univariate.html); [D011-CFA-HYPOTHESIS-TESTS-2026](https://www.cfainstitute.org/insights/professional-learning/refresher-readings/2026/hypothesis-testing) **Limit:** The arch documentation lists available forms, not their universal suitability. It does not fully cover stochastic volatility or every multivariate conditional-covariance method; these must be compared separately when the task needs them.
- **GARCH versus genuine stochastic volatility** â€” Both represent time-varying volatility but their variance dynamics differ. Standard GARCH is observation-driven, conditioning variance on observed returns/shocks; genuine stochastic-volatility models add a latent volatility innovation, making inference and estimation more complex but connecting naturally to continuous-time asset-pricing models. Empirical comparisons do not establish one winner for every asset or horizon. They are related model families whose suitability depends on data, inference and pricing/risk output, not a mandatory chain. **Sources:** [D011-CHICAGO-FED-STOCHASTIC-VOLATILITY-2009](https://www.chicagofed.org/publications/working-papers/2009/wp-04); [D011-JFUTURES-GARCH-IMPLIED-REALIZED-2016](https://www.storre.stir.ac.uk/handle/1893/23189); [D011-ARCH-VOLATILITY-PROCESSES-2026](https://arch.readthedocs.io/en/stable/univariate/volatility.html) **Limit:** The Chicago Fed paper is a 2009 conceptual review and the applied comparison is one 2016 stock-index study. Validate current methods and market-specific performance; implementation availability alone does not establish route fit.
- **Daily returns, option-implied volatility and high-frequency realized volatility** â€” These are different information sources and targets. Return-based GARCH estimates conditional variance from past returns; implied volatility is option-price-derived and forward-looking under pricing assumptions; realized volatility measures variation from intraday returns and can itself become a time series for AR/VAR/HAR-style forecasting. Sources report that combinations or relative rankings vary by horizon, proxy, loss function and market; realized and implied measures can add information rather than simply replace GARCH. **Sources:** [D011-JFUTURES-GARCH-IMPLIED-REALIZED-2016](https://www.storre.stir.ac.uk/handle/1893/23189); [D011-NBER-REALIZED-VOL-2001](https://www.nber.org/papers/w8160); [D011-CORSI-HAR-RV-2009](https://doi.org/10.1093/jjfinec/nbp001) **Limit:** Proxy construction, microstructure noise, option liquidity, sampling frequency and forecast horizon materially affect results. The cited comparisons are bounded historical markets and do not justify a universal choice or direct sequence edge.
- **HAR, long-memory/regime econometrics and ML for realized-volatility forecasts** â€” HAR-RV aggregates realized volatility across daily, weekly and monthly horizons as a parsimonious, long-memory-like baseline; ARFIMA and threshold/smooth-transition/Markov-switching variants model different persistence or regime behavior. XGBoost and neural networks offer more flexible nonlinear alternatives and can benefit from richer predictors, but two recent comparisons show no systematic ML win over the broader econometric set; forecast winner depends on horizon, predictor set and statistical versus economic objective. ML can test nonlinear extensions without replacing traditional baselines by default. **Sources:** [D011-CORSI-HAR-RV-2009](https://doi.org/10.1093/jjfinec/nbp001); [D011-FED-REALIZED-VOL-ML-COMPARE-2025-REV2026](https://www.federalreserve.gov/econres/feds/linear-and-nonlinear-econometric-models-against-machine-learning-models.htm); [D011-JEF-REALIZED-VOL-ML-2024](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jempfin.2024.101524) **Limit:** The Fed FEDS paper is preliminary author research and the peer-reviewed 2024 study covers ten equity indices and a bounded sample. Compare on task-specific rolling out-of-sample designs and guard against leakage; no model family is universally superior.
- **Risk-neutral derivative valuation differs from real-world return forecasting** â€” No-arbitrage replication/pricing values an option under a pricing measure implied by replicating portfolios or risk-neutral probabilities; it does not require predicting the underlying's actual expected return or real-world up/down probabilities. Discrete-time binomial models make hedge ratios and early-exercise decisions visible; continuous-time BSM/Black formulas are analytic models under stronger assumptions. Black is used for specified futures/rate options and swaptions. These are related pricing formulations whose fit depends on payoff, instrument and assumptions, not a sequence of statistical forecasts. **Sources:** [D011-CFA-CONTINGENT-CLAIMS-MODELS-2026](https://www.cfainstitute.org/insights/professional-learning/refresher-readings/2026/valuation-contingent-claims); [D011-CFA-CONTINGENT-CLAIMS-2026](https://www.cfainstitute.org/insights/professional-learning/refresher-readings/2026/valuation-contingent-claims); [D011-CFA-DERIVATIVES-2026-PDF](https://www.cfainstitute.org/sites/default/files/docs/programs/cfa-program/2026-l2-topics-combined.pdf); [D011-ONET-QFA-2026](https://www.onetonline.org/link/details/13-2099.01) **Limit:** The CFA pages provide curriculum-level models and applications; they do not cover every payoff or model assumption. Do not confuse a risk-neutral pricing measure with a student's statistical forecast or physical probability estimate.
- **Analytic formulas, trees, finite differences and Monte Carlo are pricing engines, not competing financial products** â€” The same contract can be priced using different calculation engines when model assumptions permit: closed-form Black/BSM for supported European structures, tree/lattice backward induction for discrete-time and exercise decisions, finite-difference grids for pricing PDEs, and Monte Carlo/pathwise simulation for path-dependent or high-dimensional payoffs. The relevant choice depends on payoff/exercise features, model, convergence/error controls and computational budget. A platform's listed engine availability is evidence of implementation, not proof an engine is accurate or best for every contract. **Sources:** [D011-QUANTLIB-OPTION-ENGINE-COMPARISON](https://github.com/lballabio/QuantLib/blob/master/Examples/EquityOption/EquityOption.cpp); [D011-OPEN-GAMMA-PRODUCT-COVERAGE](https://strata.opengamma.io/product_coverage/); [D011-CFA-CONTINGENT-CLAIMS-MODELS-2026](https://www.cfainstitute.org/insights/professional-learning/refresher-readings/2026/valuation-contingent-claims); [D011-CFA-ARBITRAGE-FREE-FIXED-INCOME-2026](https://www.cfainstitute.org/insights/professional-learning/refresher-readings/2026/arbitrage-free-valuation-framework) **Limit:** Engine coverage varies by product and software version. The code example demonstrates implementation comparison; method assumptions, convergence and product-specific numerical error need separate validation. Engine alternatives do not imply students must learn every engine in one path.
- **Yield-curve construction and calibration feed pricing, while model calibration and validation are separate** â€” Curve construction uses selected observable market instruments, conventions, discount/forward curve purposes, interpolation and a calibration measure/root finder. The calibrated curve becomes an input to pricing and curve-risk calculations. For embedded optionality or rate derivatives, a term-structure model can then be fit/used to represent rate dynamics, with a tree or Monte Carlo path valuation. Matching input instruments is curve calibration; it does not validate dynamics, extrapolation, hedging performance or model suitability. This is a technical data-to-model dependency in pricing work, not automatically a learner-level blocker edge. **Sources:** [D011-OPEN-GAMMA-RATES-CURVE-CALIBRATOR](https://strata.opengamma.io/apidocs/com/opengamma/strata/pricer/curve/RatesCurveCalibrator.html); [D011-OPEN-GAMMA-CURVE-CALIBRATION](https://strata.opengamma.io/curve_calibration_loader/); [D011-CFA-ARBITRAGE-FREE-FIXED-INCOME-2026](https://www.cfainstitute.org/insights/professional-learning/refresher-readings/2026/arbitrage-free-valuation-framework); [D011-QUANTLIBPY-TERM-STRUCTURES-2026](https://quantlib-python-docs.readthedocs.io/en/latest/termstructures.html) **Limit:** Curve nodes, day-count/calendar conventions, interpolation and multi-curve framework depend on currency, collateral, instrument liquidity and market regime. Project documentation demonstrates implementation details, not a universal methodology or data-quality certification.
- **Fixed-income pricing branches by cash-flow optionality and rate dynamics** â€” A non-option bond can be valued from cash flows discounted on the relevant spot/discount curve. Embedded or standalone rate options require modeling future rate states or paths: calibrated binomial lattices support backward induction and early exercise; pathwise/Monte Carlo simulation handles simulated rate paths and other structures. Equilibrium and arbitrage-free approaches differ in how they represent and fit the current curve. Choice depends on contractual optionality, rate exposure, calibration instruments and output needs; one tree or short-rate model is not a universal fixed-income pricer. **Sources:** [D011-CFA-ARBITRAGE-FREE-FIXED-INCOME-2026](https://www.cfainstitute.org/insights/professional-learning/refresher-readings/2026/arbitrage-free-valuation-framework); [D011-OPEN-GAMMA-PRODUCT-COVERAGE](https://strata.opengamma.io/product_coverage/); [D011-OPEN-GAMMA-RATES-CURVE-CALIBRATOR](https://strata.opengamma.io/apidocs/com/opengamma/strata/pricer/curve/RatesCurveCalibrator.html); [D011-BIS-MARKET-RISK-2026](https://www.bis.org/publications/fsi-summary-revised-market-risk-framework-executive-summary) **Limit:** CFA curriculum and platform coverage show method families, while BIS material covers banking exposure context; neither chooses a specific model for a given product. Rate model risk, negative-rate behavior, volatility surface, curve conventions and legal instrument terms need product-specific evidence.
- **Credit derivative valuation combines discounting and default-probability inputs** â€” A CDS/pricing workflow can infer a piecewise hazard/default-probability curve from quoted CDS spreads using a recovery assumption, contract conventions and a discount curve, then use survival/default probabilities and cash flows in valuation. This is distinct from a borrower credit score or a physical probability-of-default estimate: market-implied credit curves reflect market quotes and assumptions. The curve method connects derivative pricing with credit-risk modeling, but a single flat hazard rate is only a simplifying model. **Sources:** [D011-QUANTLIB-GUIDE-CREDIT-CURVES-2026](https://www.quantlibguide.com/Default-probability%20curves.html); [D011-QUANTLIBPY-TERM-STRUCTURES-2026](https://quantlib-python-docs.readthedocs.io/en/latest/termstructures.html); [D011-OPEN-GAMMA-PRODUCT-COVERAGE](https://strata.opengamma.io/product_coverage/); [D011-BIS-CREDIT-IRB](https://www.bis.org/committees/bcbs/basel-consolidated-guidelines/module/cri/30) **Limit:** The QuantLib guide is implementation instruction and the platform docs show available product families; neither establishes market-standard conventions on its own. Recovery, discounting, counterparty, liquidity, basis and ISDA documentation can materially alter the result; validate against current contract conventions.
- **Market-implied credit valuation and real-world default prediction answer different questions** â€” CVA/credit-derivative valuation uses market-implied default/survival information alongside discounted exposure and loss-given-default assumptions; a borrower's observed historical default likelihood is a different target. Regulatory material explicitly warns not to substitute risk-neutral default probabilities for observed default probabilities. This connects market spread/curve construction with derivative valuation and counterparty risk, while historical PD estimation remains a separate credit-risk task. **Sources:** [D011-BIS-CVA-CREDIT-VALUATION-FRAMEWORK](https://www.bis.org/committees/bcbs/basel-framework/standard/mar/50/inforce/2022-01-01/published/2019-12-15); [D011-BIS-CREDIT-SPREAD-STRUCTURAL-REDUCED-FORM-2004](https://www.bis.org/publ/work160.pdf); [D011-QUANTLIB-GUIDE-CREDIT-CURVES-2026](https://www.quantlibguide.com/Default-probability%20curves.html) **Limit:** The Basel passage applies to its prudential CVA framework and is subject to local adoption; the BIS paper is a dated study. Risk-neutral and physical probabilities must be identified by valuation purpose and calibration inputs, not inferred from the label alone.
- **Structural and reduced-form credit models encode different default mechanisms** â€” Structural models derive default from a firm's asset-value process crossing a default boundary, linking credit claims to option/firm-value models. Reduced-form or intensity models represent default as a random event with modeled arrival intensity and survival probabilities. Both can support bond/CDS valuation or risk analysis but require different inputs, calibration and output interpretation; neither is a universal replacement for the other. Their calibration errors and model-specification errors must be assessed separately. **Sources:** [D011-BIS-CREDIT-SPREAD-STRUCTURAL-REDUCED-FORM-2004](https://www.bis.org/publ/work160.pdf); [D011-BIS-CREDIT-MODEL-CALIBRATION-ERRORS](https://www.bis.org/publications/measuring-portfolio-credit-risk-modelling-versus-calibration-errors); [D011-BIS-CVA-CREDIT-VALUATION-FRAMEWORK](https://www.bis.org/committees/bcbs/basel-framework/standard/mar/50/inforce/2022-01-01/published/2019-12-15) **Limit:** The BIS working papers are older, bounded research and do not establish a current model winner. The CVA rule defines a regulatory output rather than endorsing one general structural or intensity model; compare against current research and task-specific validation needs.
- **Pricing-engine architecture explains alternatives without claiming accuracy** â€” An instrument definition can be kept separate from a replaceable calculation engine, allowing analytic, tree, finite-difference or Monte Carlo implementations to price the same contract when their assumptions and payoff handling fit. That is a software architecture relationship and a useful explanation of alternatives; choosing an engine still requires numerical-analysis evidence about convergence, error, stability and computational cost for the specific product. **Sources:** [D011-QUANTLIB-REPLACEABLE-PRICING-ENGINES-5](https://www.quantlib.org/quep/quep005.html); [D011-QUANTLIB-OPTION-ENGINE-COMPARISON](https://github.com/lballabio/QuantLib/blob/master/Examples/EquityOption/EquityOption.cpp); [D011-OPEN-GAMMA-PRODUCT-COVERAGE](https://strata.opengamma.io/product_coverage/) **Limit:** QuantLib's proposal and implementation examples are credible for describing that project's architecture and available examples only. They are grey project evidence, not a comparative accuracy benchmark, market-practice survey or learner prerequisite specification.
- **Portfolio allocation methods branch by investor objective, liability and constraints** â€” Mean-variance optimization is one asset-only allocation framework. Liability-relative/surplus optimization, goals-based allocation, factor allocation and risk budgeting address different objectives or risk definitions; constraints, rebalancing, taxes, liquidity, time horizon and implementation costs modify how a target portfolio is made investable. Portfolio construction therefore follows a stated objective, expected-return/risk inputs and constraints, and leads to implementation and performance evaluation; there is no single method sequence that fits every mandate. **Sources:** [D011-CFA-ASSET-ALLOCATION-2026](https://www.cfainstitute.org/insights/professional-learning/refresher-readings/2026/principles-asset-allocation); [D011-CFA-REAL-WORLD-ALLOCATION-CONSTRAINTS-2026](https://www.cfainstitute.org/insights/professional-learning/refresher-readings/2026/asset-allocation-with-real-world-constraints); [D011-CFA-ACTIVE-EQUITY-PORTFOLIO-2026](https://www.cfainstitute.org/insights/professional-learning/refresher-readings/2026/active-equity-investing-portfolio-construction) **Limit:** Current CFA curriculum is a professional teaching source, not a universal job requirement or evidence that each alternative applies to every investor. The selected objective and constraints must come from the actual portfolio mandate and governance context.
- **MVO extensions address input sensitivity and implementation limits in different ways** â€” Traditional MVO depends on expected-return and covariance estimates and can produce concentrated, input-sensitive weights. Blackâ€“Litterman combines an equilibrium prior with investor views; constraints and resampling can limit certain pathologies; robust formulations model uncertainty in estimates; shrinkage regularizes covariance inputs; risk parity changes the allocation objective; and CVaR/semi-variance objectives focus on downside loss rather than variance. These are alternative or complementary design choices, not a ladder of compulsory methods. Compare resulting weights, exposures, turnover, costs and mandate fit. **Sources:** [D011-CFA-ASSET-ALLOCATION-2026](https://www.cfainstitute.org/insights/professional-learning/refresher-readings/2026/principles-asset-allocation); [D011-STANFORD-MARKOWITZ-PRACTICAL-OPTIMIZATION-2024](https://web.stanford.edu/~boyd/papers/markowitz.html); [D011-PYPORTFOLIOOPT-OPTIMIZATION-METHODS](https://github.com/PyPortfolio/PyPortfolioOpt); [D011-CVXPY-PORTFOLIO-QUADRATIC-PROGRAM](https://www.cvxpy.org/examples/basic/quadratic_program.html) **Limit:** The CFA summary and Stanford paper identify documented limitations/extensions; PyPortfolioOpt lists software implementations rather than validating them. No alternative is universally stable or superior; empirically compare against the relevant benchmark, input uncertainty and constraints.
- **Covariance estimation branches from sample moments to shrinkage and structured factor risk models** â€” The sample covariance is the transparent baseline but can be noisy, ill-conditioned or non-invertible when the asset dimension is large relative to the observation window; error in its inverse can produce unstable optimized weights. Linear shrinkage pulls a sample estimate toward a structured target; nonlinear/quadratic eigenvalue shrinkage changes eigenvalues more selectively; factor covariance reduces the free parameters through observed or latent factors. These are alternative estimators for the covariance input, not separate allocation objectives. Choice depends on sample-to-asset ratio, dependence/regime assumptions, factor specification, mandate constraints and whether the goal is covariance accuracy or realized portfolio risk. **Sources:** [D011-LEDOIT-WOLF-SHRINKAGE-2004](https://www.ledoit.net/honey_abstract.htm); [D011-LEDOIT-WOLF-NONLINEAR-SHRINKAGE-2012](https://doi.org/10.1214/12-AOS989); [D011-LEDOIT-WOLF-QUADRATIC-SHRINKAGE-2022](https://doi.org/10.3150/20-BEJ1315); [D011-CONLON-COTTER-KYNIGAKIS-FACTOR-COVARIANCE-2025](https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S0377221725002127); [D011-CFA-ASSET-ALLOCATION-2026](https://www.cfainstitute.org/insights/professional-learning/refresher-readings/2026/principles-asset-allocation) **Role/task context:** quantitative analyst (conditional_direct): Relevant when the assignment estimates portfolio covariance, optimizes asset weights, measures portfolio risk, or builds/tests the corresponding model; an instrument-pricing-only assignment may not need portfolio covariance methods. Sources: [D011-ONET-QFA-2026](https://www.onetonline.org/link/details/13-2099.01); quantitative researcher (conditional_direct): Relevant when research builds portfolio/risk forecasts or allocation strategies; cited research-employer tasks also include financial time series and model building, but not every project is covariance or portfolio optimization. Sources: [D011-JS-QUANT-RESEARCHER-2026](https://www.janestreet.com/join-jane-street/position/8600948002/), [D011-TS-QUANT-RESEARCHER-ML-2025](https://careers.twosigma.com/careers/JobDetail/New-York-New-York-United-States-Quantitative-Researcher-Machine-Learning/12634); financial risk specialist (conditional_direct): Relevant for portfolio/market-risk estimation and model development or monitoring; select an estimator only where the actual exposure and risk model use covariance structure. Sources: [D011-ONET-13-2054.00](https://www.onetonline.org/link/summary/13-2054.00), [D011-BLACKROCK-RQA-CAREER-2026](https://careers.blackrock.com/students-and-graduates-functions-risk-quantitative-analysis); asset manager (conditional_downstream): Relevant when the manager owns or interprets portfolio allocation, risk budgeting, factor allocation or systematic construction; the occupational source does not say every manager personally estimates covariance. Sources: [D011-ONET-11-3031.03](https://www.onetonline.org/link/summary/11-3031.03); quantitative trader (conditional_downstream): Relevant if the trading mandate includes portfolio-level position sizing, covariance-aware risk limits or portfolio construction; employer evidence also covers strategy execution and risk, but does not make every trading strategy covariance-based. Sources: [D011-JS-QUANT-TRADER-2026](https://www.janestreet.com/join-jane-street/position/8573523002/); quantitative developer (conditional_implementation): Relevant when implementing, testing or operating covariance estimation and portfolio-optimization systems; implementation work does not itself choose or validate the financial estimator. Sources: [D011-P72-QUANT-DEV-TRADING-RESEARCH-2026](https://careers.point72.com/CSJobDetail?jobCode=IVS-0014934&jobName=quantitative-developer-trading-research&locale=English&location=Warsaw%2C+PL&retURL=%2FCSCareerSearch); investment analyst (conditional_downstream): Relevant when analyst duties include portfolio analytics, investment-risk measurement or systematic allocation; general valuation and recommendation duties alone do not establish covariance estimation as required. One Ford Foundation investment-analytics posting documents such duties, but its deadline passed and its direct page was unreadable. Sources: [D011-ONET-13-2051.00](https://www.onetonline.org/link/summary/13-2051.00), [D011-FORD-INVESTMENT-ANALYTICS-ANALYST-2026](https://fordfoundation.wd1.myworkdayjobs.com/en-US/FordFoundationCareerPage/job/Investment-Analytics-Analyst_R1303); statistician (conditional_method_transfer): Statistical estimation expertise transfers when assigned to a financial portfolio/risk problem; generic statistician tasks do not establish finance specialization or this technique as a role requirement. Sources: [D011-ONET-15-2041.00](https://www.onetonline.org/link/summary/15-2041.00) **Limit:** Sources establish method families and bounded results; no estimator is universally best. Covariance loss and portfolio loss are different evaluation targets, and nonstationarity, serial dependence, missing data, constraints, transaction costs and estimation windows can change rankings. Test with rolling or otherwise time-respecting out-of-sample evaluation.
- **ML can regularize, learn covariance structure or tune shrinkage inside portfolio construction** â€” Machine-learning links here are more specific than 'apply ML to finance': regularization and cross-validation can target portfolio-level estimation risk; PCA/PLS can construct lower-dimensional latent factors for covariance; reinforcement learning or supervised policy learning can tune shrinkage intensity; and recent work learns strategy/constraint-aware covariance structure. These methods extend the classical chain (returns â†’ covariance/risk estimate â†’ constrained portfolio weights â†’ cost-aware evaluation). They may be complementary to conventional estimators or alternative estimators, but they do not replace the portfolio objective, financial assumptions, comparators or validation. Compare against sample covariance, linear/nonlinear shrinkage, factor models, 1/N and relevant mandate baselines using chronological data splits, turnover/transaction costs and stability diagnostics. **Sources:** [D011-ELKAROUI-LIM-ML-PORTFOLIO-2018](https://pubsonline.informs.org/doi/10.1287/mnsc.2016.2644); [D011-CONLON-COTTER-KYNIGAKIS-FACTOR-COVARIANCE-2025](https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S0377221725002127); [D011-GOLDBERG-GURDOGAN-KERCHEVAL-EIGENVECTOR-SHRINKAGE-2025](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s00780-025-00566-4); [D011-MATTERA-RL-COVARIANCE-SHRINKAGE-2023](https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S2667305323000066); [D011-NEURAL-NONLINEAR-SHRINKAGE-2026](https://academic.oup.com/jfec/article/24/2/nbag002/8512976); [D011-QUANTCONNECT-PORTFOLIO-TO-EXECUTION-FLOW](https://www.quantconnect.com/docs/v2/writing-algorithms/algorithm-framework/overview) **Limit:** Recent ML comparisons are research proposals/results on bounded datasets, not evidence of universal or live-market superiority. The Linked QuantConnect documentation supports only the downstream software handoff, not the validity of a learned estimate. Treat any learned covariance/parameter policy as a model requiring leakage controls, independent evaluation and governance.
- **Systematic signals feed portfolio targets, risk controls and execution through distinct stages** â€” A systematic workflow can turn a universe and research signals/forecasts into target holdings, then apply portfolio risk constraints and send adjusted targets to execution. Portfolio construction is not the same as alpha research, risk management or execution: each stage consumes different inputs and yields a different artifact. Platform docs make this handoff explicit, while current portfolio-construction material shows that liquidity, turnover, market impact and mandate limits feed back into feasible weights and execution choices. **Sources:** [D011-QUANTCONNECT-PORTFOLIO-TO-EXECUTION-FLOW](https://www.quantconnect.com/docs/v2/writing-algorithms/algorithm-framework/overview); [D011-CFA-ACTIVE-EQUITY-PORTFOLIO-2026](https://www.cfainstitute.org/insights/professional-learning/refresher-readings/2026/active-equity-investing-portfolio-construction); [D011-MSCI-PORTFOLIO-CONSTRUCTION-2026](https://www.msci.com/research-and-insights/paper/the-common-language-of-portfolio-construction); [D011-FINRA-ALGORITHMIC-STRATEGY-CONTROLS](https://www.finra.org/sites/default/files/notice_doc_file_ref/Notice_Regulatory_15-09.pdf) **Limit:** QuantConnect documents its own software architecture and MSCI's 2026 source was only publicly previewed; CFA curriculum and FINRA controls have distinct scopes. The stage model is useful context, not proof of one universal architecture, canonical graph edge or method prerequisite.
- **Trade-cost measurement, execution schedules, order choice and learned execution policies are connected but distinct** â€” For a desired position or alpha signal, the learner must distinguish (a) measuring completed-trade cost from choosing an execution policy, (b) static schedules/benchmarks such as TWAP or VWAP from adaptive policies, and (c) a price-impact model from the optimizer that uses it. Implementation shortfall captures explicit cost, impact, delay and unfilled opportunity cost more fully than VWAP cost estimates, while neither benchmark is itself automatically an algorithm.
