Classical Bertsimasâ€“Lo expected-cost control and Almgrenâ€“Chriss cost/volatility optimization provide tractable baselines; market-versus-limit order models add fill probability, order-book depth and completion risk; FX practice sources describe passive, sweeping, participation and hybrid choices under different urgency/liquidity trade-offs. Supervised cost/fill prediction or RL can then estimate uncertain components or learn state-dependent actions. Recent RL papers explicitly build on classical objectives/models and report generalization, calibration, out-of-distribution and model-misspecification limits. Thus ML is a possible downstream extension of execution modeling/control, not a replacement for understanding costs, market microstructure, baseline schedules, constraints and chronological/out-of-sample evaluation. **Sources:** [D011-CFA-TCA-2026](https://www.cfainstitute.org/insights/professional-learning/refresher-readings/2026/trading-costs-and-electronic-markets); [D011-BIS-FX-EXECUTION-ALGORITHMS-2020](https://www.bis.org/publications/fx-execution-algorithms-and-market-functioning); [D011-BERTIMAS-LO-EXECUTION-1998](https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S1386418197000128); [D011-ALMGREN-CHRISS-EXECUTION-2001](https://doi.org/10.21314/JOR.2001.041); [D011-CARTEA-JAIMUNGAL-EXECUTION-ORDERS-2015](https://www.tandfonline.com/doi/abs/10.1080/14697688.2015.1032543); [D011-IJCAI-GENERALIZABLE-RL-EXECUTION-2023](https://www.ijcai.org/proceedings/2023/553); [D011-CHEN-LUDOVSKI-VOSS-ML-EXECUTION-SURROGATES-2023](https://doi.org/10.1080/14697688.2023.2282657); [D011-WANG-GAO-LI-RL-EXECUTION-2026](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s00780-026-00589-5) **Role/task context:** quantitative trader (conditional task connection): Only where the role assignment includes translating signals or target positions into orders, optimizing fills/costs, or evaluating execution; alpha research alone does not establish execution ownership. Sources: [D011-JS-QUANT-TRADER-2026](https://www.janestreet.com/join-jane-street/position/8573523002/) **Limit:** Sources range from professional curriculum and a 2020 FX institutional report to bounded research models/simulators. They establish task/method distinctions and candidate relationships, not a universal sequence, a claim that every quantitative trader implements these methods, or proof any current algorithm outperforms in live trading. The relevant quant-trader role evidence is employer-specific and only supports routes where execution is an assigned task.
- **Financial econometric estimators and empirical designs branch by data structure and research question** â€” Regression and inference foundations support several different branches. OLS, robust/clustered inference, MLE and GMM are estimation/inference choices; MLE uses a specified likelihood while GMM uses moment restrictions, and the latter includes IV-GMM rather than serving as a separate causal design. With repeated firms/assets over time, pooled OLS, fixed effects, random effects, first differences and other panel estimators rely on different assumptions; Fama–MacBeth is a two-pass cross-sectional asset-pricing procedure, not a generic substitute for panel regression. For endogeneity, IV/2SLS, LIML and IV-GMM require relevant, defensible instruments and diagnostic checks. DiD, RDD, randomized designs and selection-on-observables address causal questions under different identification assumptions. A finance event study estimates normal return and abnormal market response around an event using estimation/event windows and tests; it is a design for market reactions, not automatically a causal experiment. Select based on estimand, assignment/data structure, identifying assumptions, dependence, horizon and inference—not by method popularity. These branches can combine when a concrete design calls for it, but each connection is conditional rather than a universal sequence. **Sources:** [D011-BIRMINGHAM-FINANCIAL-ECONOMETRICS-2026](https://program-and-modules-handbook.bham.ac.uk/webhandbooks/WebHandbooks-control-servlet?Action=getModuleDetailsList&pgCrse=36331&pgSubj=07&searchTerm=002026); [D011-YORK-FINANCIAL-ECONOMETRICS-2026](https://www.york.ac.uk/students/studying/manage/programmes/module-catalogue/module/ECO00084M/2026-27?creditLevel=&department=&electiveTheme=&isAvailableAsElective=false&isAvailableToVisitingStudents=true&max=15&offset=180&query=&teachingPeriod=); [D011-NHH-FINANCIAL-ECONOMETRICS-2026](https://www.nhh.no/en/courses/financial-econometrics2/); [D011-LINEARMODELS-7-1](https://bashtage.github.io/linearmodels/); [D011-FAMA-MACBETH-1973](https://www.journals.uchicago.edu/doi/10.1086/260061); [D011-ELGHOUL-INTERNATIONAL-EVENT-STUDY-2022](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35822194/) **Role/task context:** quantitative analyst (conditional research task connection): Use these designs only when the assigned work studies financial data with a cross-sectional, panel, event-response, asset-pricing or causal estimand; a role title alone does not show that every design is used. Sources: [D011-ONET-QFA-2026](https://www.onetonline.org/link/details/13-2099.01); quantitative researcher (conditional research task connection): Relevant when the research assignment is empirical finance/asset-pricing or estimates a causal/structural parameter; the cited posting supports quantitative model/statistical research generally, not each named design individually. Sources: [D011-JS-QUANT-RESEARCHER-2026](https://www.janestreet.com/join-jane-street/position/8600948002/) **Limit:** The curricula show representative finance-method coverage and implementation docs enumerate estimator families; neither curriculum inclusion nor package availability determines a method's validity for a specific dataset. Identification and inference assumptions, market structure and design-specific diagnostics remain decisive. This note is evidence for route review, not an approved graph edge or compulsory course sequence.
- **Machine learning can extend finance prediction and econometric nuisance estimation, but ordinary prediction does not identify causality** â€” Finance ML research applies regularized regression, trees and neural nets to high-dimensional asset-pricing predictors and tests whether flexible interactions improve out-of-sample prediction against regression benchmarks. This is predictive modeling and needs time-aware evaluation; it does not by itself identify why a predictor affects returns. For causal/structural inference, double/debiased ML uses flexible learners for high-dimensional nuisance functions inside Neyman-orthogonal estimating equations with cross-fitting, while the target parameter and identification assumptions remain econometric. These are different ways to combine modern ML with traditional methods: predictive ML extends the forecast model, whereas DML extends estimation of nuisance components while preserving a design-based estimand and inference. Neither method repairs an invalid instrument, weak overlap, unmodeled dependence or a misspecified causal design. **Sources:** [D011-RFS-ML-ASSET-PRICING-2020](https://doi.org/10.1093/rfs/hhaa009); [D011-CHERNOZHUKOV-DML-2018](https://academic.oup.com/ectj/article/21/1/C1/5056401?login=false); [D011-SCIKIT-TIME-SERIES-SPLIT](https://scikit-learn.org/stable/modules/cross_validation.html) **Role/task context:** quantitative researcher (conditional research task connection): Prediction methods apply where research produces return/risk forecasts; DML applies only when the research question has an identified low-dimensional causal/structural target and valid data design. Sources: [D011-JS-QUANT-RESEARCHER-2026](https://www.janestreet.com/join-jane-street/position/8600948002/); quantitative analyst (conditional model-development connection): Use only for assigned predictive or inferential model-development tasks; the broad occupational model-development evidence does not imply that every analyst uses ML or DML. Sources: [D011-ONET-QFA-2026](https://www.onetonline.org/link/details/13-2099.01) **Limit:** Asset-pricing results are bounded to the paper's US equity data/predictors and out-of-sample protocol. DML guarantees depend on stated orthogonality, identification, convergence and dependence conditions; an ML model's predictive accuracy is not evidence of causal identification. These links are research-context candidates, not route prerequisites.

## D166 vehicle-control methods and contextual relationships

These source-backed comparisons describe technique distinctions, alternatives, complements and claim limits for later graph review. They are not canonical items, student-display approvals, or graph/sequence edges.

- **Trajectory tracking joins planning outputs to vehicle motion commands** -- The planning layer supplies a reference path/trajectory and speed profile; localization/state estimation and vehicle-status feedback supply current pose, velocity and actuator state. A tracking controller converts tracking error and the vehicle model into lateral steering and longitudinal speed/acceleration commands, then the vehicle interface must translate those into the particular vehicle's steering, throttle/brake or target-speed interface. This is a concrete input/output dependency in the control task, not a requirement that every vehicle use one architecture or one software package. **Sources:** [D166-AUTOWARE-TRAJECTORY-FOLLOWER-CONTROL](https://autowarefoundation.github.io/autoware_universe/main/control/autoware_trajectory_follower_node/); [D166-AUTOWARE-PID-LONGITUDINAL-CONTROLLER](https://autowarefoundation.github.io/autoware_universe/latest/control/autoware_pid_longitudinal_controller/); [D166-APOLLO-CONTROL-TUNING](https://github.com/ApolloAuto/apollo/blob/master/docs/10_Control/how_to_tune_control_parameters.md) **Role/task context:** software developer (conditional control-software task connection): Applies when the software assignment implements or integrates trajectory-following control, control interfaces or vehicle-specific command conversion; the general title alone is insufficient. Sources: [D166-LUCID-PLANNING-CONTROL-ENGINEER-2026-10-23](https://lucidmotors.com/careers/search/5242856007); mechanical engineer (conditional vehicle-dynamics/control-design connection): Applies where the role is assigned vehicle dynamics models, actuator/control design or vehicle motion validation; mechanical background alone does not imply AV controller work. Sources: [D166-TESLA-VEHICLE-DYNAMICS-CONTROLS-2026-10-23](https://www.tesla.com/careers/search/job/vehicle-dynamics-controls-engineer-vehicle-software-255255) **Limit:** Autoware and Apollo provide implementation examples. Vehicle interfaces, feedback availability, delay, dynamics and command semantics differ; integration and vehicle-level validation remain necessary.
- **Pure Pursuit, PID, LQR and MPC are context-dependent controller choices, not a universal ladder** -- Pure Pursuit is a geometric lateral path-tracking option; PID is a feedback family used in the documented Autoware longitudinal controller; Apollo documents an LQR lateral implementation. MPC instead repeatedly predicts vehicle motion with a model and optimizes control actions under state/input constraints, which can be useful for coupled objectives and explicit limits at the cost of model, optimization and compute requirements. These methods overlap in the broad task of tracking a trajectory but differ in control axis, formulation, assumptions and output; they are alternatives only for the same specific control problem after matching interface, vehicle model, operating range, latency and validation needs. A domain graph should preserve those conditions rather than connect them as automatic prerequisites or rank them as a fixed sequence. **Sources:** [D166-AUTOWARE-TRAJECTORY-FOLLOWER-CONTROL](https://autowarefoundation.github.io/autoware_universe/main/control/autoware_trajectory_follower_node/); [D166-AUTOWARE-MPC-LATERAL-CONTROLLER](https://autowarefoundation.github.io/autoware_universe/main/control/autoware_mpc_lateral_controller/); [D166-AUTOWARE-PID-LONGITUDINAL-CONTROLLER](https://autowarefoundation.github.io/autoware_universe/latest/control/autoware_pid_longitudinal_controller/); [D166-APOLLO-CONTROL-TUNING](https://github.com/ApolloAuto/apollo/blob/master/docs/10_Control/how_to_tune_control_parameters.md); [D166-STANO-MPC-TRACKING-REVIEW-2023](https://openresearch.surrey.ac.uk/esploro/outputs/journalArticle/Model-predictive-path-tracking-control-for/99822538402346) **Role/task context:** software developer (conditional controller implementation and integration): The Lucid posting names LQR, MPC, PID, adaptive and robust controllers for lateral/longitudinal ADAS/AV systems; that supports named-method context for a specialist task, not a general requirement for all software developers. Sources: [D166-LUCID-PLANNING-CONTROL-ENGINEER-2026-10-23](https://lucidmotors.com/careers/search/5242856007) **Limit:** The official project docs establish concrete implementations; the peer-reviewed review synthesizes MPC research through 2021. Neither proves universal controller prevalence, comparative winner or a default learning sequence. Lateral and longitudinal controller comparisons must not be conflated.
- **Learning-based control can augment a model-based controller; RL examples often solve a different control level** -- Autoware's experimental Smart MPC documents learning vehicle-model residuals from recorded driving data and using learned corrections with iLQR/MPPI trajectory following. This is a hybrid model-based/data-driven extension with data collection, train/validation separation, simulation and vehicle-specific generalization checks; the project also documents a case where model mismatch still degrades tracking. The 2024 RL review focuses on tactical highway lane changes, merges and platoon coordination. Those policies concern interaction choices and scenario-level control, and should be linked to low-level tracking only when a specific architecture shows the policy output becomes a trajectory or motion command consumed by a tracker. Neither source supports removing conventional baselines or assuming learned control is production-safe. **Sources:** [D166-AUTOWARE-SMART-MPC-LEARNING-CONTROL](https://autowarefoundation.github.io/autoware_universe/pr-10077/control/autoware_smart_mpc_trajectory_follower/); [D166-RL-HIGHWAY-CONTROL-REVIEW-2024](https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S2773153724000082); [D166-LIU-AV-PLANNING-CONTROL-CURRICULUM](https://isy.gitlab-pages.liu.se/fs/courses/TSFS12/) **Limit:** Autoware Smart MPC is a project-specific experimental implementation. The RL paper is a review of selected highway scenarios and simulation practice, not proof of robust on-road deployment or universal relevance to all AVs.
- **Safety constraints and validation complement nominal tracking control** -- Control barrier functions and related safety filters can constrain or minimally modify a nominal controller's requested action to preserve a defined safe set under stated model assumptions. Safety-oriented MPC can incorporate constraints inside an optimization. They are different integration patterns: a filter around a nominal controller versus constraints embedded in a predictive controller. The learner should see the plant/controller, safety condition, assumptions, uncertainty treatment and validation boundary; CBFs or safety-MPC labels alone do not constitute a safety case or guarantee for a real vehicle. **Sources:** [D166-CBF-SAFETY-CRITICAL-CONTROL-REVIEW-2024](https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S1367578824000166); [D166-SAFETY-MPC-AV-REVIEW-2026](https://www.mdpi.com/2673-4052/7/3/88); [D166-AVSC-ADS-SAFETY-METRICS-2021](https://go.sae.org/rs/525-RCG-129/images/AVSC00006202103.pdf) **Limit:** CBF review is broader than AVs and the 2026 AV safety-MPC review is abstract-level only because publisher retrieval was limited. AVSC is voluntary dated practice. Any guarantee is conditional on formal assumptions, implementation and vehicle/test evidence.

## D166 sensing, calibration and fusion relationships

These source-backed comparisons describe technique distinctions, alternatives, complements and claim limits for later graph review. They are not canonical items, student-display approvals, or graph/sequence edges.

- **Sensor data becomes usable downstream only after its frames, calibration, timing and uncertainty are interpretable** -- Sensor drivers and acquisition pipelines provide measurements with timestamps, coordinate frames, units and uncertainty conventions. Intrinsic calibration describes a sensor's internal measurement geometry; extrinsic calibration relates sensor frames to one another or to the vehicle; temporal calibration/synchronization aligns measurements in time. These are distinct but complementary prerequisites for spatial fusion, motion compensation and consistent perception/localization inputs. Online drift estimation can monitor changing transforms after mechanical movement or thermal/vibration change, while factory/target-based calibration and field/targetless calibration solve different setup conditions. A wrong transform or time offset can make otherwise valid detections disagree, so residual/health checks and downstream validation matter. **Sources:** [D166-ROS-REP-145-IMU-CONVENTIONS](https://ros.org/reps/rep-0145.html); [D166-AUTOWARE-SENSOR-CALIBRATION-TUTORIAL](https://docs.autoware.org/main/tutorials/integrating-autoware/creating-vehicle-and-sensor-model/calibrating-sensors/); [D166-MEASUREMENT-SENSOR-CALIBRATION-SURVEY-2026](https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S0263224126022013); [D166-IEEE-SENSOR-TEMPORAL-MISALIGNMENT-2024](https://ieeexplore.ieee.org/document/10568077/); [D166-MOBILEYE-SENSOR-CALIBRATION-2026-10-23](https://careers.mobileye.com/jobs/sensor-calibration-engineer/3e08463f-6b23-40bb-996c-c61fe1ad5e10); [D166-WAYVE-3D-SENSING-ROLE-2026-10-23](https://jobs.ashbyhq.com/wayve/08bd82d2-6896-4239-8efe-80249cc9fb90) **Role/task context:** mechatronics engineer (conditional sensor-integration/calibration connection): Relevant when assigned sensor mounting, coordinate-frame integration, data-quality checks or calibration verification; the software-heavy Mobileye role is an adjacent example, not title equivalence. Sources: [D166-MOBILEYE-SENSOR-CALIBRATION-2026-10-23](https://careers.mobileye.com/jobs/sensor-calibration-engineer/3e08463f-6b23-40bb-996c-c61fe1ad5e10), [D166-RIVIAN-AUTONOMY-SENSING-SYSTEMS-2026-10-23](https://careers.rivian.com/careers-home/jobs/29594?lang=en-us) **Limit:** ROS REP 145 is an old Draft/Informational ecosystem convention, and the 2026 survey is abstract-only/paywalled. Employer postings show particular teams' tasks, not universal process. Calibration should be treated as a technical dependency for the fusion configurations that require aligned sensor data, not an unconditional learning blocker for every sensing item or single-sensor application.
- **Camera, LiDAR, radar and inertial/GNSS sensors provide complementary measurements; no modality is a universal substitute** -- Cameras provide dense image/color/appearance cues; LiDAR provides direct range and 3D structure; radar provides range and radial-velocity information under different sensing physics; IMU and wheel/odometry signals provide high-rate ego-motion constraints, while GNSS contributes an external position reference where available. Their outputs, failure modes, range/resolution, weather sensitivities, cost and compute differ. Sensor selection is therefore an ODD, vehicle, function and redundancy trade study; fusion uses complementary measurements only when the architecture can calibrate, time-align and validate them. Radar-camera and camera-LiDAR fusion are active design families, not reasons to teach one sensor as obsolete or imply every vehicle uses the same sensor suite. **Sources:** [D166-IEEE-RADAR-CAMERA-FUSION-REVIEW-2024](https://ieeexplore.ieee.org/document/10225711/); [D166-IEEE-TRANSFORMER-SENSOR-FUSION-2025](https://experts.mcmaster.ca/scholarly-works/3554797); [D166-NIST-LIDAR-EVALUATION-2025](https://www.nist.gov/publications/methods-evaluate-3d-lidars-used-automated-driving); [D166-RIVIAN-AUTONOMY-SENSING-SYSTEMS-2026-10-23](https://careers.rivian.com/careers-home/jobs/29594?lang=en-us); [D166-NVIDIA-DRIVEWORKS-SDK](https://developer.nvidia.com/drive/driveworks); [D166-ARGOVERSE-2-DATASETS](https://www.argoverse.org/av2.html) **Limit:** Fusion surveys are abstract-level/repository-record evidence in this pass and focus on perception research; NIST study is static LiDAR testing. Sensor-performance comparison must be task/ODD-specific, use test protocols and quantitative evidence, and distinguish sensor hardware from perception models.
- **Classical filtering and geometric fusion, learned feature fusion and transformers are different integration choices** -- Classical pipelines use sensor geometry, explicit coordinate transforms, probabilistic estimators and track/object-level combination; learned pipelines may fuse raw measurements, intermediate features or object/track outputs and can learn cross-modal representations. Transformer-based fusion is one modern learned family for camera-LiDAR/radar perception, with compute and latency costs that must be measured. Early/data-level, mid/feature-level and late/object-level fusion trade information sharing, modularity and tolerance to imperfect alignment; this is an architecture choice that depends on data availability, calibration integrity, timing, real-time budget, failure handling and validation. Learned fusion builds on the measurement and geometry foundations and can coexist with explicit calibration/uncertainty monitoring; it does not make those foundations unnecessary. **Sources:** [D166-IEEE-RADAR-CAMERA-FUSION-REVIEW-2024](https://ieeexplore.ieee.org/document/10225711/); [D166-IEEE-TRANSFORMER-SENSOR-FUSION-2025](https://experts.mcmaster.ca/scholarly-works/3554797); [D166-IEEE-SENSOR-TEMPORAL-MISALIGNMENT-2024](https://ieeexplore.ieee.org/document/10568077/); [D166-ROS-REP-145-IMU-CONVENTIONS](https://ros.org/reps/rep-0145.html) **Limit:** The two fusion reviews were not fully read in this pass and the IEEE paper is abstract/index-level. Do not claim one fusion stage is best or robust to misalignment based on these sources alone; review full papers, benchmark conditions, sensor suite, compute and safety evidence before approving a specific combination or route.

## D166 localization and vehicle-state estimation relationships

These source-backed comparisons describe technique distinctions, alternatives, complements and claim limits for later graph review. They are not canonical items, student-display approvals, or graph/sequence edges.

- **Relative odometry, absolute references and map matching solve related but different localization problems** -- Wheel/IMU and LiDAR-inertial odometry estimate motion relative to a starting state and can provide high-rate continuity, but accumulated drift must be bounded. GNSS can anchor position globally when its availability and quality support the operating context; it may be degraded or unavailable. LiDAR or camera map matching estimates pose against a known map and therefore depends on map coverage, correct initialization, calibration and scene/map agreement. These inputs can complement one another in a configured estimator; they are conditional alternatives when the same pose task has different sensor, map, infrastructure or ODD constraints. The learner should see which signal anchors the frame, which propagates motion, what map is required, and how confidence/failure is reported. **Sources:** [D166-AUTOWARE-LOCALIZATION-INTERFACES](https://docs.autoware.org/main/design/autoware-architecture-v1/interfaces/components/localization/); [D166-AUTOWARE-LOCALIZATION-LAUNCH-TUTORIAL](https://docs.autoware.org/main/tutorials/integrating-autoware/launch-autoware/localization/); [D166-APOLLO-MSF-LOCALIZATION](https://github.com/ApolloAuto/apollo/blob/master/modules/localization/msf/README.md?plain=1); [D166-LIO-SAM](https://github.com/TixiaoShan/LIO-SAM); [D166-VEHICLE-LOCALIZATION-SURVEY-2023](https://ieeexplore.ieee.org/abstract/document/10261939) **Role/task context:** software developer (conditional localization implementation/integration task): Applies only where the developer's assignment configures or implements ego-motion, GNSS/map anchoring, sensor fusion or localization health outputs. Sources: [D166-AURORA-LOCALIZATION-ROLE-2026-10-23](https://aurora.tech/careers/f9fb3e3a-e5c1-418b-99c8-53e97b2f64f6), [D166-WAABI-LOCALIZATION-ROLE-2026-10-23](https://jobs.lever.co/waabi/ca67aa9c-d647-41d5-a45b-38d94304344a) **Limit:** Autoware and Apollo show particular selectable configurations; LIO-SAM is a research implementation and the IEEE survey is abstract-level. Availability, accuracy and failure behavior depend on sensors, map, vehicle, environment and ODD. This comparison does not itself approve route edges.
- **EKF filtering and factor-graph smoothing are estimator formulations that can differ in update style and scope** -- An EKF is a recursive filtering approach that updates a current state estimate as measurements arrive; Autoware's documented localizer fuses pose and twist with delay handling, bias estimation, outlier gating and covariance outputs. Factor graphs express states and sensor/map observations as constraints for joint optimization; GTSAM and LIO-SAM document this family, including IMU preintegration and smoothing/mapping. These are not simple substitutes in every system: an online filter can meet low-latency update needs, while graph optimization can revise coupled states with accumulated constraints at greater compute/data-structure cost; systems may use one, the other, or a hybrid at different stages. Selection depends on latency, sensor model, initialization, compute, observability and evaluation evidence. **Sources:** [D166-AUTOWARE-EKF-LOCALIZER](https://autowarefoundation.github.io/autoware_core/pr-248/localization/autoware_ekf_localizer/); [D166-GTSAM-FACTOR-GRAPHS](https://gtsam.org/tutorials/intro.html); [D166-LIO-SAM](https://github.com/TixiaoShan/LIO-SAM); [D166-ROBOT-LOCALIZATION-SURVEY-2026](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s41315-026-00528-9); [D166-AURORA-LOCALIZATION-ROLE-2026-10-23](https://aurora.tech/careers/f9fb3e3a-e5c1-418b-99c8-53e97b2f64f6) **Role/task context:** software developer (conditional estimator implementation and evaluation): The Aurora and Waabi examples explicitly name state estimation/Kalman or factor-graph work; this supports specialist task context only, not requirements for every software developer. Sources: [D166-AURORA-LOCALIZATION-ROLE-2026-10-23](https://aurora.tech/careers/f9fb3e3a-e5c1-418b-99c8-53e97b2f64f6), [D166-WAABI-LOCALIZATION-ROLE-2026-10-23](https://jobs.lever.co/waabi/ca67aa9c-d647-41d5-a45b-38d94304344a) **Limit:** The Autoware, GTSAM and LIO-SAM sources document implementations/tutorials, not a controlled head-to-head benchmark. The broad 2026 survey covers robotics generally. Do not teach a universal EKF-before-factor-graph ladder or infer comparative performance without primary studies and matched test conditions.
- **Pose, covariance, status and health checks are outputs needed by downstream components** -- Localization supplies an estimated vehicle state/pose, and configured systems may also provide covariance, confidence/status and diagnostic signals. Downstream planning and control consume state estimates to relate the vehicle to routes and trajectories; their behavior must account for stale, uncertain or invalid estimates. Covariance should not be treated as ground-truth uncertainty unless its estimator assumptions and calibration are validated: Autoware explicitly documents limitations in its NDT covariance modifier. Health monitoring, timeouts, consistency checks and safe system response connect estimator outputs to downstream operation; a pose estimate alone does not establish that a route is safe or that a full safety case exists. **Sources:** [D166-AUTOWARE-EKF-LOCALIZER](https://autowarefoundation.github.io/autoware_core/pr-248/localization/autoware_ekf_localizer/); [D166-AUTOWARE-NDT-SCAN-MATCHER](https://autowarefoundation.github.io/autoware.universe_planning/pr-5583/localization/ndt_scan_matcher/); [D166-AUTOWARE-POSE-COVARIANCE-MODIFIER](https://autowarefoundation.github.io/autoware_universe/latest/localization/autoware_pose_covariance_modifier/); [D166-APOLLO-MSF-LOCALIZATION](https://github.com/ApolloAuto/apollo/blob/master/modules/localization/msf/README.md?plain=1); [D166-APOLLO-LOCALIZATION-MODULE](https://github.com/ApolloAuto/apollo/blob/master/modules/localization/README.md) **Limit:** These sources establish implementation outputs and selected diagnostics, not a complete AV safety architecture. The downstream relationship is functional context; exact contracts and safety responses must be established per stack and validated against applicable standards/ODD.
- **Learned localization methods are emerging options that augment specific components; they are not a default replacement for geometry and estimation** -- The 2026 robotics survey includes semantic and learned localization approaches alongside classical estimation, while Autoware's current launcher documents a camera-based YabLoc option and a separate NDT map-matching path. These establish more than one implementation family but do not show that a learned estimator universally replaces calibrated sensor geometry, map references, uncertainty checks or conventional baselines. Learned components should be compared on their target failure mode, training/evaluation distribution, localization integrity, latency and ODD coverage. A student should encounter the option after understanding state, frames, measurement models, drift and validation, with any claimed complement or alternative kept tied to a named configuration and evidence. **Sources:** [D166-ROBOT-LOCALIZATION-SURVEY-2026](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s41315-026-00528-9); [D166-AUTOWARE-LOCALIZATION-LAUNCH-TUTORIAL](https://docs.autoware.org/main/tutorials/integrating-autoware/launch-autoware/localization/); [D166-WAABI-LOCALIZATION-ROLE-2026-10-23](https://jobs.lever.co/waabi/ca67aa9c-d647-41d5-a45b-38d94304344a); [D166-AV-LOCALIZATION-SURVEY-2024](https://www.mdpi.com/2075-1702/12/2/118) **Limit:** The broad survey is robotics-wide; the AV-specific 2024 review was inaccessible beyond abstract/index in this pass; YabLoc is a project option and Waabi lists learned localization only as a bonus. Evidence supports an emerging option, not general adoption or superiority.

## D166 automated-driving planning relationships

These source-backed comparisons describe technique distinctions, alternatives, complements and claim limits for later graph review. They are not canonical items, student-display approvals, or graph/sequence edges.

- **Mission route, behavior choice, path and time-based trajectory are related stages with different outputs** -- A destination and map support a mission/global route. Local planning then uses route/map context with current localized vehicle state and perceived/predicted agents to select or revise behavior (such as follow, yield, lane-change or stop), construct a path/drivable region, and generate a time-indexed trajectory with position, speed and acceleration for control. Validation/diagnostics can inspect candidate and selected trajectories before control consumes them. This is a common modular pattern, not an immutable universal stack: some architectures combine behavior and trajectory generation, and receding-horizon/MPC planners may include control decisions directly. Teach the terms by each stage's input/output and handoff, not as synonyms. **Sources:** [D166-AUTOWARE-PLANNING-DESIGN-2026](https://docs.autoware.org/main/design/autoware-architecture-v1/components/planning/); [D166-APOLLO-PLANNING-COMPONENT-CURRENT](https://github.com/ApolloAuto/apollo/blob/master/docs/08_Planning/planning_component.md); [D166-HYBRID-MOTION-PLANNING-SURVEY-2024](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.05575) **Role/task context:** software developer (conditional planning stack implementation and interface task): Applies to developer assignments implementing behavior, path/trajectory generation or planning interfaces; role title alone does not imply these tasks. Sources: [D166-AURORA-BEHAVIOR-PLANNING-ROLE-2026-10-23](https://aurora.tech/careers/877f468f-dabd-4c33-aced-99dcd7517e2c), [D166-DIDI-MOTION-PLANNING-ROLE-2026-10-23](https://job-boards.greenhouse.io/didi/jobs/8299704) **Limit:** Autoware/Apollo are two implementation examples and the hybrid survey describes modular patterns; architectures differ and some planners merge layers. Do not turn this typical flow into a hard universal sequence or graph blocker without checking the target design.
- **Search, sampling, optimization and interpolation are method families for different planning subproblems; hybrid combinations are common** -- Graph/search methods (including A*/Dijkstra and lattice-style search) explore discretized or structured candidate spaces; sampling methods (for example RRT-family approaches) explore candidate states/paths; optimization methods shape a feasible path or trajectory against costs and constraints; splines/interpolation can represent or smooth candidate geometry. Vehicle kinematics/dynamics, collision checking, map/drivable bounds, traffic rules, agent uncertainty, timing and compute budget change which method is suitable. These families can be alternatives when solving the same defined path/trajectory task, but hybrid systems may use search or sampling to produce candidates and optimization to refine/rank them. None is a universal prerequisite for another, and a method label without its representation, constraints and objective is not enough to choose. **Sources:** [D166-APOLLO-PLANNING-PLUGIN-CATALOG](https://github.com/ApolloAuto/apollo/blob/master/collection/planning/README.md); [D166-AUTOWARE-PLANNING-DESIGN-2026](https://docs.autoware.org/main/design/autoware-architecture-v1/components/planning/); [D166-ADS-PATH-PLANNING-SURVEY-2024](https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S0921889024000137); [D166-HYBRID-MOTION-PLANNING-SURVEY-2024](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.05575); [D166-SAMPLING-PLANNING-REVIEW-2024](https://doi.org/10.1146/annurev-control-061623-094742) **Role/task context:** software developer (conditional method implementation choice): Waabi, Gatik and Torc postings name different algorithm families for their specialist planning work; choose based on the assigned vehicle model, ODD and planner contract, not the title alone. Sources: [D166-WAABI-MOTION-PLANNING-ROLE-2026-10-23](https://jobs.lever.co/waabi/39f0345f-177c-4feb-8c27-7de26645eb14), [D166-GATIK-MOTION-PLANNING-ROLE-2026-10-23](https://job-boards.greenhouse.io/gatikaiinc/jobs/4063226006?gh_jid=4063226006), [D166-TORC-MOTION-PLANNING-ROLE-2026-10-23](https://job-boards.greenhouse.io/torcrobotics/jobs/8613575002?gh_src=e8tj8mqp2us) **Limit:** The ADS path-planning survey is abstract-level and the sampling review is broader robotics; employer postings show named methods at particular teams. This supports taxonomy and conditional examples, not comparative rankings or universal industry prevalence.
- **Rule-based, learned and end-to-end planning differ in what they learn and where the safety/trajectory contracts sit** -- Rule/state-machine planners explicitly encode scenario logic and behavior; learning-based planners may learn behavior or trajectory proposals from demonstrations (imitation learning), optimize policies through reinforcement learning, or learn selected components such as prediction, costs, candidate ranking or perception-to-planning representations. End-to-end methods may map sensor inputs directly to motion/planning outputs, reducing some module boundaries while making interpretation, attribution and validation harder. Interactive prediction-planning goes beyond a one-way predicted-agent input by modeling how surrounding agents react to the AV's candidate behavior. Hybrid designs can combine learned proposals or predictions with analytic constraints, optimization, rule logic and independent checks. The required dependency is the actual data/output contract of that design, not a generic claim that machine learning always follows a planner or replaces it. **Sources:** [D166-APOLLO-PLANNING-COMPONENT-CURRENT](https://github.com/ApolloAuto/apollo/blob/master/docs/08_Planning/planning_component.md); [D166-HYBRID-MOTION-PLANNING-SURVEY-2024](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.05575); [D166-INTERACTIVE-PREDICTION-PLANNING-SURVEY-2026](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10462-026-11604-8); [D166-E2E-DRIVING-SURVEY-2024](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39078757/) **Role/task context:** software developer (conditional planning/ML integration task): Aurora's posting asks for motion-planning/ML integration into verifiable systems; use as a specific employer example, not a general prerequisite for planning work. Sources: [D166-AURORA-BEHAVIOR-PLANNING-ROLE-2026-10-23](https://aurora.tech/careers/877f468f-dabd-4c33-aced-99dcd7517e2c) **Limit:** Apollo explicitly labels its documented E2E mode exploratory/not road-tested; surveys describe research and future directions. Do not claim production readiness, safety or superiority from this evidence. Prediction is a distinct upstream input unless a joint architecture models reciprocal interaction.
- **Safety constraints and planner evaluation complement planning algorithms and do not certify the whole vehicle** -- Formal safety assumptions/models such as the proposed RSS rules and IEEE 2846's scoped assumptions can inform behavior constraints or safety-related planner checks, but neither replaces perception/prediction quality, vehicle dynamics, implementation assurance or a complete system safety case. Open-loop trajectory similarity and closed-loop simulation answer different questions: closed-loop evaluation lets the proposed trajectory alter ego state and may include reactive agents, collisions, road compliance, progress and comfort; simulation still cannot establish real-world safety by itself. A useful planner comparison therefore records scenario coverage, agent reactivity, map/sensor assumptions, controller, metrics, compute and real-world validation boundaries. **Sources:** [D166-RSS-ORIGINAL-PAPER-2017](https://arxiv.org/abs/1708.06374); [D166-IEEE-2846-BEHAVIOR-SAFETY](https://sagroups.ieee.org/2846/); [D166-MOBILEYE-RSS-PROPOSAL](https://www.mobileye.com/technology/responsibility-sensitive-safety/); [D166-NUPLAN-OFFICIAL-BENCHMARK](https://www.nuplan.org/); [D166-NUPLAN-DEVKIT-EVALUATION](https://github.com/motional/nuplan-devkit/blob/master/docs/competition.md?plain=1); [D166-AVSC-ADS-SAFETY-METRICS-2021](https://go.sae.org/rs/525-RCG-129/images/AVSC00006202103.pdf) **Limit:** RSS is a proponent-originated model; IEEE summary explicitly excludes uncertainty sources and states it does not guarantee whole-system safety. nuPlan is a bounded research simulator and NHTSA's 2017 voluntary guidance is not a current comprehensive approval regime. No source here establishes a safety guarantee for a specific AV.

## D166 high-definition mapping relationships

These source-backed comparisons describe technique distinctions, alternatives, complements and claim limits for later graph review. They are not canonical items, student-display approvals, or graph/sequence edges.

- **Semantic lane maps, point-cloud maps and simulation road formats carry different kinds of road knowledge** -- A point-cloud map stores measured 3D surface/geometry and can support scan matching or map-relative perception; a vector/semantic lane map stores road/lane boundaries, topology, directions, traffic controls and participant-specific rules used for routing and behavior; some systems pair both. Lanelet2 is an operational map framework with participant-aware routing and traffic-rule interpretation. Apollo has a separate protobuf lane model. ASAM OpenDRIVE describes a static road network primarily for simulation exchange; OpenCRG adds detailed road-surface profiles and OpenSCENARIO describes dynamic actors/maneuvers that reference the static road network. These formats overlap in road representation but are not interchangeable substitutes: decide from consumer, required geometry/semantics, coordinate system, topology, update mechanism and conversion needs. A file extension or standard label alone does not prove the map has correct, fresh or complete content. **Sources:** [D166-AUTOWARE-MAP-INTERFACE-2026](https://docs.autoware.org/main/design/autoware-architecture-v1/interfaces/components/map/); [D166-AUTOWARE-MAP-CREATION-TUTORIAL-2026](https://docs.autoware.org/main/tutorials/integrating-autoware/creating-maps/); [D166-LANELET2-MAP-FRAMEWORK-PRIMARY](https://github.com/fzi-forschungszentrum-informatik/Lanelet2); [D166-APOLLO-MAP-LANE-SCHEMA](https://github.com/ApolloAuto/apollo/blob/master/modules/common_msgs/map_msgs/map_lane.proto); [D166-ASAM-OPENDRIVE-2026](https://www.asam.net/standards/detail/opendrive/); [D166-ASAM-OPENCRG-ROAD-SURFACE](https://www.asam.net/standards/detail/opencrg/); [D166-ASAM-OPENSCENARIO-STATIC-DYNAMIC-CONTENT](https://www.asam.net/fileadmin/Standards/OpenSCENARIO/QUICK_READ_ASAM_OpenSCENARIO_BS-1-2_User-Guide_V1-0-0.html) **Role/task context:** software developer (conditional map data-model/interface task): Zoox, GM and map-infrastructure employer examples describe data formats, geometry/topology and downstream interfaces; apply when a developer owns the map schema or consumer contract. Sources: [D166-ZOOX-MAPPING-DATA-ROLE-2026-10-23](https://jobs.lever.co/zoox/8102c816-7ce1-49c8-8093-f46c8074652c), [D166-GM-SENIOR-SOFTWARE-MAPPING-ROLE-2026-10-23](https://generalmotors.wd5.myworkdayjobs.com/en-US/Careers_GM/job/Senior-Machine-Learning-Engineer---Mapping_JR-202620459-1), [D166-APPLIED-MAPS-INFRASTRUCTURE-ROLE-2026-10-23](https://jobs.ashbyhq.com/applied/366ad3c0-3988-4a94-a825-8fe6238a2a9a/) **Limit:** These sources document specific formats/implementations. OpenDRIVE is simulation-oriented; Lanelet2 and Apollo schema details are implementation-specific, and conversion may lose/alter semantics. Do not present any one schema as the universal AV map standard.
- **Map creation combines surveying or SLAM acquisition with georeferencing, feature extraction, semantics, topology and quality checks** -- A defensible map-production workflow begins with a use case, ODD, coordinate reference and required accuracy/semantic detail; acquires data through mobile mapping/survey, vehicle sensors or other surveyed sources; uses calibrated pose and sensor data to align observations; reconstructs geometry and extracts/annotates lanes, boundaries, signs, signals, crossings and other required features; attaches traffic/legal semantics and connects topological relations; converts into the chosen consumer schema; and validates geometric accuracy, semantic consistency, connectivity and downstream behavior before publishing a version. Manual editing, mobile-mapping surveys, SLAM-based point-cloud reconstruction and automated camera/LiDAR/ML extraction are context-dependent approaches that can be combined. A quick single-lane map from recorded pose is a prototype route, not a substitute for a complete production map. **Sources:** [D166-AUTOWARE-MAP-CREATION-TUTORIAL-2026](https://docs.autoware.org/main/tutorials/integrating-autoware/creating-maps/); [D166-TSUSHIMA-ETAL-HD-MAP-PRODUCTION-2020](https://isprs-archives.copernicus.org/articles/XLIII-B4-2020/415/2020/); [D166-LANELET2-MAP-VALIDATION](https://docs.ros.org/en/ros2_packages/humble/api/lanelet2_validation/__README.html); [D166-ASRAT-CHO-HD-MAP-SURVEY-2024](https://www.mdpi.com/2220-9964/13/7/232); [D166-HDMAP-TECHNOLOGY-REVIEW-2025](https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S2667325824000268); [D166-ARGOVERSE-HD-MAP-API](https://argoverse.github.io/user-guide/api/hd_maps.html) **Role/task context:** software developer (conditional map reconstruction, data pipeline or QA implementation): GM, NVIDIA and Applied Intuition examples describe distinct algorithm, infrastructure and map-authoring tasks; attach only the task branch actually assigned. Sources: [D166-GM-STAFF-ML-MAPPING-ROLE-2026-10-23](https://search-careers.gm.com/en/jobs/jr-202602984/staff-machine-learning-engineer-mapping/), [D166-NVIDIA-MAPPING-SOFTWARE-ROLE-2026-10-23](https://nvidia.wd5.myworkdayjobs.com/en-US/NVIDIAExternalCareerSite/job/US-CA-Santa-Clara/Systems-Software-Engineer--Autonomous-Systems-Mapping---New-College-Graduate-2026_JR2020838), [D166-APPLIED-MAPPING-SPECIALIST-ROLE-2026-10-23](https://jobs.ashbyhq.com/applied/cd80b2b9-d375-4050-b85e-ec6bb82a575c/), [D166-APPLIED-MAPS-INFRASTRUCTURE-ROLE-2026-10-23](https://jobs.ashbyhq.com/applied/366ad3c0-3988-4a94-a825-8fe6238a2a9a/) **Limit:** Autoware and Lanelet2 provide practical examples; the production paper dates to 2020 and the two recent surveys are abstract/index-level here. The staged workflow synthesizes task families; do not treat it as a mandated single sequence or infer production accuracy without survey control, error budgets and acceptance criteria.
- **Map maintenance is a change-detection, evidence-fusion and controlled-release problem, not simply repeated map drawing** -- Road geometry and controls change, so fleets or survey teams can compare new sensor observations with a stored map, localize candidate discrepancies, aggregate corroborating observations, estimate confidence/quality, distinguish persistent infrastructure change from transient objects or construction, update affected geometry/semantics/topology, validate the changed region and dependent routes, version and release the update, and monitor its consumers. Crowdsourced fleet observations, remote sensing, vehicle camera/LiDAR/radar, human map editing and end-to-end learned lane/topology generation are possible data or automation sources under different latency, coverage, noise, privacy and trust assumptions. Research datasets can benchmark selected discrepancies; they do not establish a universal automatic-release policy. Map output needs provenance, temporal validity, quality gates and rollback/monitoring suitable to its use. **Sources:** [D166-ARGOVERSE-MAP-CHANGE-DATASET](https://argoverse.github.io/user-guide/datasets/map_change_detection.html); [D166-ARGOVERSE-HD-MAP-API](https://argoverse.github.io/user-guide/api/hd_maps.html); [D166-CROWD-MAP-QUALITY-2026](https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S1569843226002670); [D166-E2E-MAP-GENERATION-SURVEY-2026](https://jst.tsinghuajournals.com/EN/10.16511/j.cnki.qhdxxb.2026.28.018); [D166-ASRAT-CHO-HD-MAP-SURVEY-2024](https://www.mdpi.com/2220-9964/13/7/232); [D166-HDMAP-TECHNOLOGY-REVIEW-2025](https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S2667325824000268) **Role/task context:** software developer (conditional freshness/change-detection and map lifecycle task): GM, Waabi, PlusAI and Caterpillar postings describe map changes or create/validate/maintain systems; Caterpillar's listing is expired and mining-specific, and PlusAI combines mapping with localization. Sources: [D166-GM-STAFF-ML-MAPPING-ROLE-2026-10-23](https://search-careers.gm.com/en/jobs/jr-202602984/staff-machine-learning-engineer-mapping/), [D166-WAABI-MAPPING-SOFTWARE-ROLE-2026-10-23](https://jobs.lever.co/waabi/0e0ba69c-ea22-4bff-ac40-8e3e5674ff90), [D166-PLUSAI-MAPPING-LOCALIZATION-ROLE-2026-10-23](https://www.plus.ai/careers/527722d7-5887-4c96-9363-46eb3ea5cbc5/), [D166-CATERPILLAR-AUTONOMY-MAP-ROLE-2026-10-23](https://careers.caterpillar.com/en/jobs/r0000389353/autonomy-map-engineer/) **Limit:** The two 2026 papers and 2025 review are abstract/index-level in this pass; Argoverse is a bounded six-city dataset. Industry posting claims are company-specific. This note supports maintenance task context, not a validated autonomous update architecture or safe-release guarantee.
- **Map geometry, topology, semantics and version/freshness metadata serve localization, perception, routing/planning and simulation through different contracts** -- A map can be consumed as a geometric localization reference, a semantic prior for object or lane understanding, a participant-aware topology/traffic-rule graph for route and behavior planning, or a static road network for simulation. Consumers need compatible frames/projections, spatial resolution, element attributes, topology, map version/time and update interfaces. Autoware, Argoverse, Lanelet2 and Apollo expose different data contracts; OpenDRIVE can supply static simulation road-network content while OpenSCENARIO provides dynamic test actors. When a consumer finds an unavailable/stale/corrupt map or a data contract mismatch, its behavior must be considered separately from the map producer's quality check. The mapping area supports these consumers but does not own their localization, perception, planning or simulation algorithms. **Sources:** [D166-AUTOWARE-MAP-INTERFACE-2026](https://docs.autoware.org/main/design/autoware-architecture-v1/interfaces/components/map/); [D166-ARGOVERSE-HD-MAP-API](https://argoverse.github.io/user-guide/api/hd_maps.html); [D166-LANELET2-ROUTING-AND-RULES](https://github.com/fzi-forschungszentrum-informatik/Lanelet2/blob/master/lanelet2_routing/README.md); [D166-APOLLO-MAP-LANE-SCHEMA](https://github.com/ApolloAuto/apollo/blob/master/modules/common_msgs/map_msgs/map_lane.proto); [D166-ASAM-OPENDRIVE-2026](https://www.asam.net/standards/detail/opendrive/); [D166-ASAM-OPENSCENARIO-STATIC-DYNAMIC-CONTENT](https://www.asam.net/fileadmin/Standards/OpenSCENARIO/QUICK_READ_ASAM_OpenSCENARIO_BS-1-2_User-Guide_V1-0-0.html) **Role/task context:** software developer (conditional map-serving/data-consumer interface task): Zoox and Waabi examples cover developer/training/on-vehicle map interfaces; PlusAI covers map query and localization use. Apply only when the software assignment owns those interfaces. Sources: [D166-ZOOX-MAPPING-DATA-ROLE-2026-10-23](https://jobs.lever.co/zoox/8102c816-7ce1-49c8-8093-f46c8074652c), [D166-WAABI-MAPPING-SOFTWARE-ROLE-2026-10-23](https://jobs.lever.co/waabi/0e0ba69c-ea22-4bff-ac40-8e3e5674ff90), [D166-PLUSAI-MAPPING-LOCALIZATION-ROLE-2026-10-23](https://www.plus.ai/careers/527722d7-5887-4c96-9363-46eb3ea5cbc5/) **Limit:** These sources provide stack and dataset examples, not one shared interoperability contract. Mapping-source records document the data responsibility; they do not redefine ownership or sequencing for the consuming areas.

## Open learning, research and practitioner resources

These resources are indexed for discovery or instruction. They are kept separate from domain/area fit evidence and from evidence that a method or strategy is valid.

### D011

## D166 interface and integration relationships

### The vehicle interface translates between normalized ADS intent and platform-specific actuation/status contracts

Planning and control may describe target motion in normalized terms, while a particular drive-by-wire platform accepts its own steering, acceleration, brake, gear or other command representation. An interface adapter translates commands, normalizes returned status, exposes supported capabilities, and must account for feedback semantics and vehicle-specific calibration. This is an integration boundary: it connects control to hardware and returns state to localization/monitoring; it does not replace trajectory generation or controller design. Apollo's schema illustrates typed command identity and extensible operations, while Autoware documents command conversion and feedback separately.

**Limits:** These are two open-source stack examples, not an interchangeable or universal vehicle protocol. Actual command semantics, timing, calibration, actuator limits, diagnostics and safety mechanisms are platform and ODD dependent.

**Conditional role context — software developer:** conditional ADS-to-vehicle integration and interface implementation. NVIDIA and Rivian examples describe cross-subsystem/vehicle integration and diagnosis; apply only when the developer owns a command/status adapter, vehicle bring-up, interface contract or comparable integration task. The NVIDIA official posting text was search-indexed and the Rivian detail is a secondary listing mirror.

**Sources:** `D166-AUTOWARE-VEHICLE-INTERFACE-DESIGN-2026`, `D166-AUTOWARE-RAW-VEHICLE-CMD-CONVERTER-2026`, `D166-APOLLO-CHASSIS-COMMAND-SCHEMA-2026`, `D166-APOLLO-SOFTWARE-ARCH-2017`

### Integration verification must observe command-to-actuation timing, feedback, capability mismatches and degraded/manual modes

A command that is syntactically accepted is not proof that the intended vehicle behavior occurred. Integration work needs to correlate command identity/time with returned actuation and vehicle status, measure conversion and end-to-end latency, detect unsupported features, diagnose sensor/compute/vehicle faults, and define what happens on disengagement or loss of valid feedback. The component boundary may support manual override and capability reporting, but the system safety argument and test evidence remain cross-stack responsibilities. Standards recommendations and service-diagnostic interfaces can inform the test contract, but their edition, scope and adoption must be checked for the target vehicle and jurisdiction.

**Limits:** Autoware documents example capabilities and a latency measurement; ITU-T F.749.17 is scoped to purpose-built ADS-dedicated vehicles. No listed source alone defines a complete safety case, response-time budget or regulatory compliance determination.

**Conditional role context — software analyst:** conditional integration-test, fault-triage and release-validation task. The current software-analysis role evidence includes AV integration and quality/safety operations examples; this connection applies only to analysis/testing assignments that inspect cross-module or vehicle-level behavior, not to every software analyst role.

**Sources:** `D166-AUTOWARE-VEHICLE-INTERFACE-DESIGN-2026`, `D166-AUTOWARE-RAW-VEHICLE-CMD-CONVERTER-2026`, `D166-ITU-T-UNIVERSAL-ADS-INTERFACES-2025`, `D166-ASAM-SOVD-1-0-0`, `D166-ISO-14229-1-2026-UDS`

## D166 safety verification and validation relationships

### Verification evidence grows across scenario, software, hardware and vehicle tests; each stage answers different questions

Scenario-based virtual tests can exercise repeatable nominal, boundary and safety-critical situations at scale; MIL/SIL/HIL benches add different levels of model, software, ECU, network and hardware interaction; closed-course and on-road evidence reveal vehicle/environment effects that simulation may omit. Scenario generation and risk prioritization can be rule-based, data-driven or increasingly learned/generative, but generated cases still need plausibility, coverage and outcome checks. ASAM OSI and XIL standardize selected data/testbench interfaces that make some components or cases portable; they do not make results equivalent across simulators or prove the real-world system safe. Combine evidence by traceability to requirements, ODD, hazards and acceptance criteria, with explicit limits and residual risks.

**Limits:** Interface standards do not establish simulator realism or safety. The scenario survey is a non-peer-reviewed abstract-only preprint; the risk review is secondary evidence; regulatory requirements are jurisdiction- and vehicle/function-specific.

**Conditional role context — software analyst:** conditional scenario-based validation, metric design, and software/vehicle regression evidence. GM autonomy-evaluation and Waymo software-quality postings describe simulation/road evaluation, scenario or dataset coverage, statistical/ML metrics, issue triage and release evidence. Apply only where the assignment owns validation or quality analysis; postings do not prove universal duties.

**Sources:** `D166-ASAM-OSI-3-8-0`, `D166-ASAM-XIL-3-0-0`, `D166-NUPLAN-OFFICIAL-BENCHMARK`, `D166-NHTSA-ADS-CRASH-ORDER-2025`, `D166-UNECE-UNR157-ALKS`, `D166-RISK-ASSESSMENT-SURVEY-2025`, `D166-SAFETY-CRITICAL-SCENARIO-SURVEY-PREPRINT-2026`
